(\gamma_{1}^{k}\cos(k\theta_{1}),\gamma_{1}^{k}\sin(k\theta_{1}),\dots,\gamma_{s}^{k}\cos(k\theta_{s}),\gamma_{s}^{k}\sin(k\theta_{s}))
, for
1\leq k\leq\delta_{p}
,
\boldsymbol{D}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\lambda})=\diag\{\lambda_{1}^{\delta_{p}},\dots,\lambda_{r}^{\delta_{p}}\}
is an
r\times r
diagonal matrix, and
\boldsymbol{D}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\eta})=\diag\{\boldsymbol{B}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{1},\delta_{p}),\dots,\boldsymbol{B}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{s},\delta_{p})\}
is a
2s\times 2s
block diagonal matrix whose
m
th block is

\boldsymbol{B}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{m},\delta_{p})=\left(\begin{matrix}\gamma_{m}^{\delta_{p}}\cos(\delta_{p}\theta_{m})&\gamma_{m}^{\delta_{p}}\sin(\delta_{p}\theta_{m})\\
-\gamma_{m}^{\delta_{p}}\sin(\delta_{p}\theta_{m})&\gamma_{m}^{\delta_{p}}\cos(\delta_{p}\theta_{m})\end{matrix}\right)\quad\text{for}\quad 1\leq m\leq s.

In particular, when
\delta_{r}=0
or
\delta_{s}=0
, the corresponding zero rows in
\boldsymbol{\overline{R}}_{1}^{\pazocal{M}}
and
\boldsymbol{\overline{R}}_{2}^{\pazocal{M}}(\cdot)
will disappear. When
\delta_{p}=0
,
\boldsymbol{L}_{1}(\cdot)
and
\boldsymbol{L}_{2}(\cdot)
will disappear, while
\boldsymbol{D}_{1}(\cdot)=\boldsymbol{I}_{r}
and
\boldsymbol{D}_{2}(\cdot)=\boldsymbol{I}_{2s}
, and then
\boldsymbol{\overline{R}}_{2}^{\pazocal{M}}(\cdot)
will reduce to the constant block diagonal matrix,
\boldsymbol{\overline{R}}_{2}^{\pazocal{M}}=\diag\{\boldsymbol{I}_{\overline{p}},\boldsymbol{\overline{R}}_{1}^{\pazocal{M}}\}
.

By Lemma
S15
, for any
\pazocal{M}=(p,r,s)\in\mathscr{M}
, the following constraints are satisfied by
\boldsymbol{\overline{\omega}}
and
\overline{\boldsymbol{G}}_{k}
for
1\leq k\leq\overline{d}
:

\overline{\lambda}_{r+1}=\cdots=\overline{\lambda}_{\overline{r}}=0,\quad\boldsymbol{\overline{\eta}}_{s+1}=\cdots=\boldsymbol{\overline{\eta}}_{\overline{s}}=\boldsymbol{0},

(S5)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}\boldsymbol{\overline{G}}_{p+k}&=\sum_{j=1}^{\overline{r}}\overline{\lambda}_{j}^{k-\delta_{p}}\boldsymbol{\overline{G}}_{\overline{p}+j}+\sum_{m=1}^{\overline{s}}\overline{\gamma}_{m}^{k-\delta_{p}}\cos\{(k-\delta_{p})\overline{\theta}_{m}\}\boldsymbol{\overline{G}}_{\overline{p}+\overline{r}+2m-1}\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\sum_{m=1}^{\overline{s}}\overline{\gamma}_{m}^{k-\delta_{p}}\sin\{(k-\delta_{p})\overline{\theta}_{m}\}\boldsymbol{\overline{G}}_{\overline{p}+\overline{r}+2m}\quad\text{for}\quad 1\leq k\leq\delta_{p},\\
\boldsymbol{\overline{G}}_{\overline{p}+r+1}&=\cdots=\boldsymbol{\overline{G}}_{\overline{p}+\overline{r}}=\boldsymbol{0},\quad\boldsymbol{\overline{G}}_{\overline{p}+\overline{r}+2s+1}=\cdots=\boldsymbol{\overline{G}}_{\overline{p}+\overline{r}+2\overline{s}}=\boldsymbol{0}.\end{split}

(S6)

These constraints can be written in vector form as

\boldsymbol{\overline{C}}_{1}^{\pazocal{M}}\boldsymbol{\overline{\omega}}=\boldsymbol{0}\quad\text{and}\quad\left(\boldsymbol{\overline{C}}_{2}^{\pazocal{M}}(\boldsymbol{\overline{\omega}})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N^{2}}\right)\boldsymbol{\overline{g}}=\boldsymbol{0}.

(S7)

Here
\boldsymbol{\overline{C}}_{1}^{\pazocal{M}}\in\mathbb{R}^{(\delta_{r}+2\delta_{s})\times(\overline{r}+2\overline{s})}
is a constant matrix encoding the
(\delta_{r}+2\delta_{s})
constraints on
\boldsymbol{\overline{\omega}}
as stated in (
S5
),

\boldsymbol{\overline{C}}_{1}^{\pazocal{M}}=\left(\begin{matrix}\boldsymbol{0}_{\delta_{r}\times r}&\boldsymbol{I}_{\delta_{r}}&\boldsymbol{0}&\boldsymbol{0}\\
\boldsymbol{0}&\boldsymbol{0}&\boldsymbol{0}_{2\delta_{s}\times 2s}&\boldsymbol{I}_{2\delta_{s}}\end{matrix}\right),

and
\boldsymbol{\overline{C}}_{2}^{\pazocal{M}}:(-1,1)^{\overline{r}}\times\boldsymbol{\varPi}^{\overline{s}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{\delta_{d}\times\overline{d}}
encodes the
\delta_{d}
constraints on
\boldsymbol{\overline{g}}
for any given
\boldsymbol{\overline{\omega}}
as stated in (
S6
),

\boldsymbol{\overline{C}}_{2}^{\pazocal{M}}(\boldsymbol{\overline{\omega}})=\left(\begin{matrix}\boldsymbol{0}_{\delta_{p}\times p}&\boldsymbol{I}_{\delta_{p}}&\boldsymbol{L}_{3}(\boldsymbol{\overline{\lambda}})&\boldsymbol{0}&\boldsymbol{L}_{4}(\boldsymbol{\overline{\eta}})&\boldsymbol{0}\\
\boldsymbol{0}&\boldsymbol{0}&\boldsymbol{0}&\boldsymbol{I}_{\delta_{r}}&\boldsymbol{0}&\boldsymbol{0}\\
\boldsymbol{0}&\boldsymbol{0}&\boldsymbol{0}&\boldsymbol{0}&\boldsymbol{0}&\boldsymbol{I}_{2\delta_{s}}\end{matrix}\right),

where
\boldsymbol{L}_{3}(\boldsymbol{\overline{\lambda}})
is a
\delta_{p}\times r
matrix whose
k
th row is
(\overline{\lambda}_{1}^{k-\delta_{p}},\dots,\overline{\lambda}_{r}^{k-\delta_{p}})
, and
\boldsymbol{L}_{4}(\boldsymbol{\overline{\eta}})
is a
\delta_{p}\times 2s
matrix whose
k
th row is

(\overline{\gamma}_{1}^{k-\delta_{p}}\cos\{(k-\delta_{p})\overline{\theta}_{1}\},\overline{\gamma}_{1}^{k-\delta_{p}}\sin\{(k-\delta_{p})\overline{\theta}_{1}\},\dots,\overline{\gamma}_{s}^{k-\delta_{p}}\cos\{(k-\delta_{p})\overline{\theta}_{s}\},\overline{\gamma}_{s}^{k-\delta_{p}}\sin\{(k-\delta_{p})\overline{\theta}_{s}\}),

for
1\leq k\leq\delta_{p}
.
Note that
\boldsymbol{\overline{C}}_{1}^{\pazocal{M}}
and
\boldsymbol{\overline{C}}_{2}^{\pazocal{M}}(\cdot)
are intrinsically determined by
\boldsymbol{\overline{R}}_{1}^{\pazocal{M}}
and
\boldsymbol{\overline{R}}_{2}^{\pazocal{M}}(\cdot)
in (
S4
), respectively. In fact, it holds

\boldsymbol{L}_{3}(\boldsymbol{\overline{\lambda}})=\boldsymbol{L}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\lambda})\boldsymbol{D}_{1}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\lambda})\quad\text{and}\quad\boldsymbol{L}_{4}(\boldsymbol{\overline{\eta}})=\boldsymbol{L}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\eta})\boldsymbol{D}_{2}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\eta}),

since

\overline{\lambda}_{j}=\mathbb{I}_{\{1\leq j\leq r\}}\lambda_{j}
for
1\leq j\leq\overline{r}
, and

\boldsymbol{\overline{\eta}}_{m}=\mathbb{I}_{\{1\leq m\leq s\}}\boldsymbol{\eta}_{m}
for
1\leq m\leq\overline{s}
.

As indicated by (
S7
), increasing
p
by one amounts to deleting a particular row from
\boldsymbol{\overline{C}}_{2}^{\pazocal{M}}(\boldsymbol{\overline{\omega}})
, while increasing
r
(or
s
) by one is equivalent to deleting a particular row (or a pair of rows) from both
\boldsymbol{\overline{C}}_{1}^{\pazocal{M}}
and
\boldsymbol{\overline{C}}_{2}^{\pazocal{M}}(\boldsymbol{\overline{\omega}})
. The following proposition is a direct consequence of the above discussion. It also establishes the monotonicity of
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\pazocal{M}}
in
\pazocal{M}
along a single direction of
p,r
or
s
.

Proposition S4

(Restricted parameter spaces)
.

Any model (
2.4
) with orders
\pazocal{M}=(p,r,s)\in\mathscr{M}
can be reparameterized as the model with orders
\overline{\pazocal{M}}=(\overline{p},\overline{r},\overline{s})
and the corresponding parameter vectors
\boldsymbol{\overline{\omega}}
and
\overline{\boldsymbol{g}}
belonging to the restricted parameter space,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\pazocal{M}}

\displaystyle=\left\{\boldsymbol{\overline{\omega}}\in(-1,1)^{\overline{r}}\times\boldsymbol{\varPi}^{\overline{s}},\;\boldsymbol{\overline{g}}\in\mathbb{R}^{N^{2}\overline{d}}:\boldsymbol{\overline{C}}_{1}^{\pazocal{M}}\boldsymbol{\overline{\omega}}=\boldsymbol{0}\text{ and }(\boldsymbol{\overline{C}}_{2}^{\pazocal{M}}(\boldsymbol{\overline{\omega}})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N^{2}})\boldsymbol{\overline{g}}=\boldsymbol{0}\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\{\boldsymbol{\overline{\omega}}=\boldsymbol{\overline{R}}_{1}^{\pazocal{M}}\boldsymbol{\omega},\;\overline{\boldsymbol{g}}=(\boldsymbol{\overline{R}}_{2}^{\pazocal{M}}(\boldsymbol{\omega})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N^{2}})\boldsymbol{g}:\boldsymbol{\omega}\in(-1,1)^{r}\times\boldsymbol{\varPi}^{s}\text{ and }\boldsymbol{g}\in\mathbb{R}^{N^{2}d}\right\}.

Moreover,

\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\pazocal{M}}\subset\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\pazocal{M}^{\prime}}
, for any
\pazocal{M}^{\prime}
obtained by increasing one of the
p,r,s
in
\pazocal{M}
by one.

S7.4
Proof of Theorem
4

In this proof, we will focus on the JE, since the proof for the RE will be similar.
Since
\overline{p},\overline{r}
and
\overline{s}
are assumed to be fixed,
\mathscr{M}
contains a fixed number of candidate models.
To prove this theorem, it suffices to show that for each
\pazocal{M}\in\mathscr{M}_{\textup{over}}\cup\mathscr{M}_{\textup{mis}}
,

\mathbb{P}\left\{\textup{BIC}({\pazocal{M}})>\textup{BIC}(\pazocal{M}^{*})\right\}\to 0\quad\text{as}\quad T\to\infty,

where

\mathscr{M}_{\textup{over}}=\{\pazocal{M}\in\mathscr{M}\mid p\geq p^{*},r\geq r^{*}\text{ and }s\geq s^{*}\}\setminus\pazocal{M}^{*}

and

\mathscr{M}_{\textup{mis}}=\{\pazocal{M}\in\mathscr{M}\mid p<p^{*},r<r^{*}\text{ or }s<s^{*}\}
.
For any
\pazocal{M}=(p,r,s)\in\mathscr{M}
, define the unregularized population minimizer:

(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{\circ}_{\pazocal{M}},\boldsymbol{g}^{\circ}_{\pazocal{M}})=\argmin_{\boldsymbol{\omega}\in(-1,1)^{r}\times\boldsymbol{\varPi}^{s},\boldsymbol{g}\in\mathbb{R}^{N^{2}d}}\mathbb{E}\{\mathbb{L}_{T}(\boldsymbol{\omega},\boldsymbol{g})\}.

Note that when
\pazocal{M}=\pazocal{M}^{*}
, we simply have
(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{\circ}_{\pazocal{M}},\boldsymbol{g}^{\circ}_{\pazocal{M}})=(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*},\boldsymbol{g}^{*})
. In addition, denote

\widetilde{\varphi}_{T,\pazocal{M}}=\tau_{N}\left[\frac{\log\{N(p\vee 1)\}}{T}\right]^{1-q/2}.

Let
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\omega}}
and
\widehat{\boldsymbol{g}}
denote the estimators obtained from fitting the correctly specified model, i.e.,
\pazocal{M}^{*}
.
Note that

\textup{BIC}(\pazocal{M})-\textup{BIC}(\pazocal{M}^{*})=\log\left(1+\frac{D_{\pazocal{M}}}{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}_{T}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\omega}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{g}})}\right)+(d\widetilde{\varphi}_{T,\pazocal{M}}-d^{*}\widetilde{\varphi}_{T,\pazocal{M}^{*}})\log T,

(S8)

where

D_{\pazocal{M}}=\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}_{T}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\omega}}_{\pazocal{M}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{g}}_{\pazocal{M}})-\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}_{T}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\omega}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{g}})=D_{\pazocal{M},1}-D_{\pazocal{M}^{*},2}+D_{\pazocal{M},3},

with
D_{\pazocal{M},1}=\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}_{T}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\omega}}_{\pazocal{M}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{g}}_{\pazocal{M}})-\mathbb{E}\{\mathbb{L}_{T}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{\circ}_{\pazocal{M}},\boldsymbol{g}^{\circ}_{\pazocal{M}})\}
,
D_{\pazocal{M}^{*},2}=\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}_{T}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\omega}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{g}})-\mathbb{E}\{\mathbb{L}_{T}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*},\boldsymbol{g}^{*})\}
, and
D_{\pazocal{M},3}=\mathbb{E}\{\mathbb{L}_{T}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{\circ}_{\pazocal{M}},\boldsymbol{g}^{\circ}_{\pazocal{M}})\}-\mathbb{E}\{\mathbb{L}_{T}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*},\boldsymbol{g}^{*})\}
.
By the proof of Theorem
2
or
3
, we can directly show that

D_{\pazocal{M}^{*},2}=O_{p}(N\widetilde{\varphi}_{T,\pazocal{M}^{*}}).

(S9)

Recall that
\boldsymbol{a}=\vect(\boldsymbol{A})
, where
\boldsymbol{A}=(\boldsymbol{A}_{1},\boldsymbol{A}_{2},\dots)
is the horizontal concatenation of
\{\boldsymbol{A}_{h}\}_{h=1}^{\infty}
. Note that
\boldsymbol{a}=(\boldsymbol{L}(\boldsymbol{\omega})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N^{2}})\boldsymbol{g}
. Throughout our proof, we will suppress the dependence of
\boldsymbol{L}(\cdot)
on
\pazocal{M}
for simplicity. Analogously, for any
\pazocal{M}\in\mathscr{M}
, we can define
\boldsymbol{\widehat{a}}_{\pazocal{M}}=\vect(\boldsymbol{\widehat{A}}_{\pazocal{M}})=(\boldsymbol{L}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\omega}_{\pazocal{M}}})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N^{2}})\boldsymbol{\widehat{g}}_{\pazocal{M}}
and
\boldsymbol{a}_{\pazocal{M}}^{\circ}=\vect(\boldsymbol{A}_{\pazocal{M}}^{\circ})=(\boldsymbol{L}(\boldsymbol{\omega}_{\pazocal{M}}^{\circ})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N^{2}})\boldsymbol{g}_{\pazocal{M}}^{\circ}
.
Moreover, by Proposition
S4
, we can write

\mathbb{E}\{\mathbb{L}_{T}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{\circ}_{\pazocal{M}},\boldsymbol{g}^{\circ}_{\pazocal{M}})\}=\mathbb{E}\{\|\boldsymbol{y}_{t}-(\boldsymbol{x}_{t}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N})\boldsymbol{a}_{\pazocal{M}}^{\circ}\|_{2}^{2}\}=\min_{(\boldsymbol{\omega},\boldsymbol{g})\in\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\pazocal{M}}}\mathbb{E}\left\{\|\boldsymbol{y}_{t}-(\boldsymbol{x}_{t}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N})\boldsymbol{a}(\boldsymbol{\omega},\boldsymbol{g})\|_{2}^{2}\right\}.

(i) Misspecified models:

Let
\pazocal{M}\in\mathscr{M}_{\textup{mis}}
. The key of this analysis is to derive a lower bound for
D_{\pazocal{M},3}
based on Proposition
S4
and then show that it dominates both
D_{\pazocal{M},1}
and
D_{\pazocal{M}^{*},2}
.

Denote

\mathscr{L}(\boldsymbol{a})=\mathbb{E}\{\|\boldsymbol{y}_{t}-(\boldsymbol{x}_{t}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N})\boldsymbol{a}\|_{2}^{2}\}
. By Lemma
S18
,
\lambda_{\min}\left\{\mathbb{E}(\boldsymbol{x}_{t}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}^{\top})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\right\}=\lambda_{\min}\left\{\mathbb{E}(\boldsymbol{x}_{t}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}^{\top})\right\}\geq\kappa_{1}
. Then, by the Taylor expansion and Proposition
S4
, we have

\displaystyle D_{\pazocal{M},3}=\mathscr{L}(\boldsymbol{a}_{\pazocal{M}}^{\circ})-\mathscr{L}(\boldsymbol{a}^{*})

\displaystyle=(\boldsymbol{a}_{\pazocal{M}}^{\circ}-\boldsymbol{a}^{*})^{\top}\left\{\mathbb{E}(\boldsymbol{x}_{t}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}^{\top})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\right\}(\boldsymbol{a}_{\pazocal{M}}^{\circ}-\boldsymbol{a}^{*})

\displaystyle\geq\kappa_{1}\|\boldsymbol{a}_{\pazocal{M}}^{\circ}-\boldsymbol{a}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\geq\delta_{\pazocal{M}},

where
\delta_{\pazocal{M}}=\kappa_{1}\inf_{(\boldsymbol{\omega},\boldsymbol{g})\in\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\pazocal{M}}}\|(\boldsymbol{L}(\boldsymbol{\omega})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N^{2}})\boldsymbol{g}-\boldsymbol{a}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}
. Note that by Assumption
7
(i) and the boundedness of
d^{*}
, we have
\delta_{\pazocal{M}}\gg Nd^{*}\widetilde{\varphi}_{T,\pazocal{M}^{*}}\log T
. As a result, it follows from (
S9
) that

D_{\pazocal{M}^{*},2}=o_{p}(\delta_{\pazocal{M}})
.
Moreover,
Assumption
7
(ii) implies

D_{\pazocal{M},1}=o_{p}(\delta_{\pazocal{M}})
.

Lastly, since
\log(1+x)\geq\min\{0.5x,\log 2\}
for any
x>0
and
\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}_{T}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\omega}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{g}})=E(\|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\|_{2}^{2})+D_{\pazocal{M}^{*},2}=O_{p}(N)
, by combining (
S8
) with the results above, we can show that

\textup{BIC}(\pazocal{M})-\textup{BIC}(\pazocal{M}^{*})\geq\min\left\{\frac{0.5D_{\pazocal{M}}}{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}_{T}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\omega}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{g}})},\log 2\right\}+(d\widetilde{\varphi}_{T,\pazocal{M}}-d^{*}\widetilde{\varphi}_{T,\pazocal{M}^{*}})\log T>0,

as
T\rightarrow\infty
.

(ii) Overspecified models:

Let
\pazocal{M}\in\mathscr{M}_{\textup{over}}
. First, we can show that

\min_{\boldsymbol{a}\in\mathbb{R}^{\infty}}\mathbb{E}\left\{\|\boldsymbol{y}_{t}-(\boldsymbol{x}_{t}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N})\boldsymbol{a}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}=\mathbb{E}\{\|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\}

and this minimum is attained at
\boldsymbol{a}^{*}=\boldsymbol{a}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*},\boldsymbol{g}^{*})
. Moreover, since
(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*},\boldsymbol{g}^{*})\in\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\pazocal{M}^{*}}\subset\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\pazocal{M}}
, we have

\mathbb{E}\{\mathbb{L}_{T}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{\circ}_{\pazocal{M}},\boldsymbol{g}^{\circ}_{\pazocal{M}})\}=\min_{(\boldsymbol{\omega},\boldsymbol{g})\in\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\pazocal{M}}}\mathbb{E}\left\{\|\boldsymbol{y}_{t}-(\boldsymbol{x}_{t}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N})\boldsymbol{a}(\boldsymbol{\omega},\boldsymbol{g})\|_{2}^{2}\right\}=\mathbb{E}\{\|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\}
,
with the minimum attained at some
(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{\circ}_{\pazocal{M}},\boldsymbol{g}^{\circ}_{\pazocal{M}})
such that
\boldsymbol{a}_{\pazocal{M}}^{\circ}=\boldsymbol{a}^{*}
.
Thus,

D_{\pazocal{M},3}=0.

(S10)

In addition, we can show that

D_{\pazocal{M},1}=O_{p}(N\widetilde{\varphi}_{T,\pazocal{M}}).

(S11)

Since
\boldsymbol{A}_{\pazocal{M}}^{\circ}=\boldsymbol{A}^{*}
, by the optimality of
\boldsymbol{\widehat{A}}_{\pazocal{M}}
, we have

\frac{3}{4T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}}_{\pazocal{M}}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}-S_{3}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}}_{\pazocal{M}})\leq\frac{2}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}}_{\pazocal{M}}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}\rangle+\lambda_{g}(\|\boldsymbol{g}^{*}\|_{1}-\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{g}}_{\pazocal{M}}\|_{1})+S_{2}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}}_{\pazocal{M}})-S_{1}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}}_{\pazocal{M}}),

where
\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}}_{\pazocal{M}}=\boldsymbol{\widehat{A}}_{\pazocal{M}}-\boldsymbol{A}^{*}
, and
S_{i}(\cdot)
for
1\leq i\leq 3
are defined as in the proof of Theorem
2
. The remainder of the proof can be completed by modifying that of Theorem
2
.
This involves adapting Proposition
2
for
\pazocal{M}\in\mathscr{M}_{\textup{over}}
.
To this end, we define the following notations: Let

\boldsymbol{g}_{\pazocal{M}}=(\boldsymbol{g}_{\pazocal{M},{\mathrm{AR}}}^{\top},\boldsymbol{g}_{\pazocal{M},{\mathrm{MA}}}^{\top})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{N^{2}d}
, where
\boldsymbol{g}_{\pazocal{M},{\mathrm{AR}}}=\vect((\boldsymbol{G}_{1},\dots,\boldsymbol{G}_{p}))
and
\boldsymbol{g}_{\pazocal{M},{\mathrm{MA}}}=\vect((\boldsymbol{G}_{p+1},\dots,\boldsymbol{G}_{d}))
. We can partition any
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{\pazocal{M}}\in(-1,1)^{r}\times\boldsymbol{\varPi}^{s}
into two subvectors:
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{\pazocal{M}^{*}}\in(-1,1)^{r^{*}}\times\boldsymbol{\varPi}^{s^{*}}
and
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{\pazocal{M}^{\delta}}\in(-1,1)^{\delta_{r}}\times\boldsymbol{\varPi}^{\delta_{s}}
, where
\delta_{r}=r-r^{*}
and
\delta_{s}=s-s^{*}
. Accordingly, partition
\boldsymbol{g}_{\pazocal{M},{\mathrm{MA}}}
into two subvectors:
\boldsymbol{g}_{\pazocal{M}^{*},{\mathrm{MA}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{N^{2}(r+2s)}
and
\boldsymbol{g}_{\pazocal{M}^{\delta},{\mathrm{MA}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{N^{2}(\delta_{r}+2\delta_{s})}
.
Then,
let
\boldsymbol{a}_{\pazocal{M},{\mathrm{AR}}}=\vect((\boldsymbol{A}_{1},\dots,\boldsymbol{A}_{p}))
and
\boldsymbol{a}_{\pazocal{M},{\mathrm{MA}}}=\vect((\boldsymbol{A}_{p+1},\boldsymbol{A}_{p+2},\dots))
.

Note that
\boldsymbol{a}_{\pazocal{M},{\mathrm{AR}}}=\boldsymbol{g}_{\pazocal{M},{\mathrm{AR}}}
and
\boldsymbol{a}_{\pazocal{M},{\mathrm{MA}}}=(\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N^{2}})\boldsymbol{g}_{\pazocal{M},{\mathrm{MA}}}=(\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega}_{\pazocal{M}^{*}})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N^{2}})\boldsymbol{g}_{\pazocal{M}^{*},{\mathrm{MA}}}+\boldsymbol{a}_{\pazocal{M}^{\delta},{\mathrm{MA}}}
, where
\boldsymbol{a}_{\pazocal{M}^{\delta},{\mathrm{MA}}}=(\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega}_{\pazocal{M}^{\delta}})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N^{2}})\boldsymbol{g}_{\pazocal{M}^{\delta},{\mathrm{MA}}}
.
By a method similar to that for deriving (
S4
), we can show that
\boldsymbol{\omega}^{\circ}_{\pazocal{M}^{\delta}}=\boldsymbol{0}
and
\boldsymbol{g}^{\circ}_{\pazocal{M}^{\delta},{\mathrm{MA}}}=\boldsymbol{0}
, which are subvectors of
\boldsymbol{\omega}^{\circ}_{\pazocal{M}}
and
\boldsymbol{g}^{\circ}_{\pazocal{M}}
, respectively.
Thus,
\boldsymbol{a}_{\pazocal{M}^{\delta},{\mathrm{MA}}}^{\circ}=(\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega}_{\pazocal{M}^{\delta}}^{\circ})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N^{2}})\boldsymbol{g}_{\pazocal{M}^{\delta},{\mathrm{MA}}}^{\circ}=\boldsymbol{0}
.
Then, by adapting the proof of Proposition
2
, under Assumptions
1
(i) and
2
, we can show that if
\|\boldsymbol{\omega}_{\pazocal{M}^{*}}-\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*}\|_{2}\leq c_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}
, then
\|\boldsymbol{a}_{\pazocal{M}^{\delta},{\mathrm{MA}}}\|_{2}+\|\boldsymbol{g}_{\pazocal{M},{\mathrm{AR}}}-\boldsymbol{g}_{\pazocal{M},{\mathrm{AR}}}^{\circ}\|_{2}+\|\boldsymbol{g}_{\pazocal{M}^{*},{\mathrm{MA}}}-\boldsymbol{g}_{\pazocal{M}^{*},{\mathrm{MA}}}^{\circ}\|_{2}+\underline{\alpha}_{\mathrm{MA}}\|\boldsymbol{\omega}_{\pazocal{M}^{*}}-\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*}\|_{2}\lesssim\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\pazocal{M}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\lesssim\|\boldsymbol{a}_{\pazocal{M}^{\delta},{\mathrm{MA}}}\|_{2}+\|\boldsymbol{g}_{\pazocal{M},{\mathrm{AR}}}-\boldsymbol{g}_{\pazocal{M},{\mathrm{AR}}}^{\circ}\|_{2}+\|\boldsymbol{g}_{\pazocal{M}^{*},{\mathrm{MA}}}-\boldsymbol{g}_{\pazocal{M}^{*},{\mathrm{MA}}}^{\circ}\|_{2}+\overline{\alpha}_{\mathrm{MA}}\|\boldsymbol{\omega}_{\pazocal{M}^{*}}-\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*}\|_{2}
. Along the lines of this adaptation, we can modify the proof of Theorem
2
to show that

D_{\pazocal{M},1}\lesssim\left[\frac{\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\log\{N(p\vee 1)\}}{\kappa_{1}^{2}T}\right]^{1-q/2}\frac{R_{q}}{\kappa_{1}^{1-q}}\lesssim\widetilde{\varphi}_{T,\pazocal{M}},

with high probability, and hence (
S11
), provided that
\boldsymbol{\widehat{\omega}}_{\pazocal{M}}
contains a subvector
\boldsymbol{\widehat{\omega}}_{\pazocal{M}^{*}}
satisfying
\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\omega}}_{\pazocal{M}^{*}}-\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*}\|_{2}\leq c_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}
.

Now using the inequality
\log(1+x)\leq x
, we have

\log\left(1+\frac{D_{\pazocal{M}}}{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}_{T}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\omega}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{g}})}\right)\geq-\frac{D_{\pazocal{M}}}{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}_{T}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\omega}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{g}})}.

Additionally, note that
\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}_{T}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\omega}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{g}})=\mathbb{E}\{\mathbb{L}_{T}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*},\boldsymbol{g}^{*})\}+D_{\pazocal{M}^{*},2}=E(\|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\|_{2}^{2})+D_{\pazocal{M}^{*},2}
, where
E(\|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\|_{2}^{2})\asymp N
. Finally, since
\widetilde{\varphi}_{T,\pazocal{M}}>\widetilde{\varphi}_{T,\pazocal{M}^{*}}
, it follows from (
S8
)–(
S11
) that

\displaystyle\textup{BIC}(\pazocal{M})-\textup{BIC}(\pazocal{M}^{*})

\displaystyle\geq(d\widetilde{\varphi}_{T,\pazocal{M}}-d^{*}\widetilde{\varphi}_{T,\pazocal{M}^{*}})\log T-O_{p}(N(\widetilde{\varphi}_{T,\pazocal{M}}-\widetilde{\varphi}_{T,\pazocal{M}^{*}})/N)

\displaystyle=(d-d^{*})\widetilde{\varphi}_{T,\pazocal{M}}\log T+O_{p}((\widetilde{\varphi}_{T,\pazocal{M}}-\widetilde{\varphi}_{T,\pazocal{M}^{*}})(d^{*}\log T-1))>0,

as
T\rightarrow\infty
.
The proof of this theorem is complete.

S8
Proofs of auxiliary lemmas

S8.1
Proof of Lemma
S2

By definition,
\ell_{h}^{I}(\lambda_{j})=\lambda_{j}^{h}
for
1\leq j\leq r
, and
\ell_{h}^{II,1}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{m})=\gamma_{m}^{h}\cos(h\theta_{m})
and
\ell_{h}^{II,2}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{m})=\gamma_{m}^{h}\sin(h\theta_{m})
for
1\leq m\leq s
.
Then their first-order derivatives are
\nabla\ell_{h}^{I}(\lambda_{j})=h\lambda_{j}^{h-1}
,
\nabla_{\gamma}\ell_{h}^{II,1}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{m})=h\gamma_{m}^{h-1}\cos(h\theta_{m})
,

\nabla_{\theta}\ell_{h}^{II,1}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{m})=-h\gamma_{m}^{h}\sin(h\theta_{m})
,
\nabla_{\gamma}\ell_{h}^{II,2}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{m})=h\gamma_{m}^{h-1}\sin(h\theta_{m})
, and

\nabla_{\theta}\ell_{h}^{II,2}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{m})=h\gamma_{m}^{h}\cos(h\theta_{m})
. Their second-order derivatives are

\nabla^{2}\ell_{h}^{I}(\lambda_{j})=h(h-1)\lambda_{j}^{h-2}
,

\nabla^{2}_{\gamma}\ell_{h}^{II,1}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{m})=h(h-1)\gamma_{m}^{h-2}\cos(h\theta_{m})
,

\nabla^{2}_{\gamma\theta}\ell_{h}^{II,1}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{m})=-h^{2}\gamma_{m}^{h-1}\sin(h\theta_{m})
,
\nabla^{2}_{\theta}\ell_{h}^{II,1}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{m})=-h^{2}\gamma_{m}^{h}\cos(h\theta_{m})
,

\nabla^{2}_{\gamma}\ell_{h}^{II,2}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{m})=h(h-1)\gamma_{m}^{h-2}\sin(h\theta_{m})
,

\nabla^{2}_{\gamma\theta}\ell_{h}^{II,2}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{m})=h^{2}\gamma_{m}^{h-1}\cos(h\theta_{m})
, and
\nabla^{2}_{\theta}\ell_{h}^{II,2}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{m})=-h^{2}\gamma_{m}^{h}\sin(h\theta_{m})
. By Assumption
1
(i), there exists
\rho_{1}>0
such that
\max\{|\lambda_{1}|,\ldots,|\lambda_{r}|,\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{s}\}\leq\rho_{1}<\bar{\rho}
. Thus,

\max_{1\leq j\leq r,1\leq m\leq s,\iota=1,2}\left\{|\nabla\ell_{h}^{I}(\lambda_{j})|,\|\nabla\ell_{h}^{II,\iota}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{m})\|_{2},|\nabla^{2}\ell_{h}^{I}(\lambda_{j})|,\|\nabla^{2}\ell_{h}^{II,\iota}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{m})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\right\}\leq C_{\ell}\bar{\rho}^{h}.

by choosing
C_{\ell}
dependent on
\rho_{1}
and
\bar{\rho}
such that
C_{\ell}\geq 2h^{2}(\rho_{1}/\bar{\rho})^{h-2}\bar{\rho}^{-2}
for all
h\geq 1
. Note that such a
0<C_{\ell}<\infty
exists and is an absolute constant.

S8.2
Proof of Lemma
S3

For simplicity, we omit the superscript “*” in all notations below. Consider the following partitions of the
\infty\times(p+J)
matrix
\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}(\boldsymbol{\omega})
:

\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}(\boldsymbol{\omega})=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\boldsymbol{I}_{p}&\boldsymbol{0}\\
\boldsymbol{0}&\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega})\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{matrix}\boldsymbol{I}_{p}&\boldsymbol{0}\\
\boldsymbol{0}&\boldsymbol{L}_{[1:J]}(\boldsymbol{\omega})\\
\boldsymbol{0}&\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{Rem}}(\boldsymbol{\omega})\end{matrix}\right),

where
\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega})=\left(\boldsymbol{L}^{I}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}),\boldsymbol{L}^{II}(\boldsymbol{\eta}),\nabla\boldsymbol{L}^{I}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}),\nabla_{\theta}\boldsymbol{L}^{II}(\boldsymbol{\eta})\right)
is further partitioned into two blocks, the
J\times J
block

\boldsymbol{L}_{[1:J]}(\boldsymbol{\omega})
and the
\infty\times J
remainder block
\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{Rem}}(\boldsymbol{\omega})
. Note that for
1\leq h\leq J
, the
h
th row of
\boldsymbol{L}_{[1:J]}(\boldsymbol{\omega})
is

\boldsymbol{L}_{h}(\boldsymbol{\omega}):=\left(\left(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{h}^{I}(\boldsymbol{\lambda})\right)^{\top},\left(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{h}^{II}(\boldsymbol{\eta})\right)^{\top},\left(\nabla\boldsymbol{\ell}_{h}^{I}(\boldsymbol{\lambda})\right)^{\top},\left(\nabla_{\theta}\boldsymbol{\ell}_{h}^{II}(\boldsymbol{\eta})\right)^{\top}\right),

where
\boldsymbol{\ell}_{h}^{I}(\boldsymbol{\lambda})=(\lambda_{1}^{h},\dots,\lambda_{r}^{h})^{\top}
,
\nabla\boldsymbol{\ell}_{h}^{I}(\boldsymbol{\lambda})=(h\lambda_{1}^{h-1},\dots,h\lambda_{r}^{h-1})^{\top}
, and

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\ell}_{h}^{II}(\boldsymbol{\eta})

\displaystyle=\left(\gamma_{1}^{h}\cos(h\theta_{1}),\gamma_{1}^{h}\sin(h\theta_{1}),\dots,\gamma_{s}^{h}\cos(h\theta_{s}),\gamma_{s}^{h}\sin(h\theta_{s})\right)^{\top},

\displaystyle\nabla_{\theta}\boldsymbol{\ell}_{h}^{II}(\boldsymbol{\eta})

\displaystyle=\left(-h\gamma_{1}^{h}\sin(h\theta_{1}),h\gamma_{1}^{h}\cos(h\theta_{1}),\dots,-h\gamma_{s}^{h}\sin(h\theta_{s}),h\gamma_{s}^{h}\cos(h\theta_{s})\right)^{\top}.

For
h\geq 1
, the
h
th row of
\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{Rem}}(\boldsymbol{\omega})
is
\boldsymbol{L}_{J+h}(\boldsymbol{\omega})
.

By Lemma
S2
, we have
\|\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\sqrt{J\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}C_{L}^{2}\bar{\rho}^{2h}}\leq C_{L}\sqrt{J}\bar{\rho}(1-\bar{\rho})^{-1}=C_{\bar{\rho}}
. Then

\displaystyle\sigma_{\max}(\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}(\boldsymbol{\omega}))\leq\max\left\{1,\sigma_{\max}(\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega}))\right\}\leq\max\left\{1,\|\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\right\}\leq\max\{1,C_{\bar{\rho}}\}

(S1)

and

\displaystyle\sigma_{\max}(\boldsymbol{L}_{[1:J]}(\boldsymbol{\omega}))\leq\|\boldsymbol{L}_{[1:J]}(\boldsymbol{\omega})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\|\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq C_{\bar{\rho}}.

(S2)

It remains to derive a lower bound of
\sigma_{\min}(\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}(\boldsymbol{\omega}))
. To this end, we first derive a lower bound of
\sigma_{\min}(\boldsymbol{L}_{[1:J]}(\boldsymbol{\omega}))
by lower bounding the determinant of
\boldsymbol{L}_{[1:J]}(\boldsymbol{\omega})
.
For any
(\gamma,\theta)\in[0,1)\times(-\pi/2,\pi/2)
, it can be verified that

\left(\gamma^{h}\cos(h\theta),\gamma^{h}\sin(h\theta)\right)\underbrace{\left(\begin{matrix}1&1\\
i&-i\end{matrix}\right)}_{:=\boldsymbol{C}_{1}}=\left((\gamma e^{i\theta})^{h},(\gamma e^{-i\theta})^{h}\right)

and

\left(-h\gamma^{h}\sin(h\theta),h\gamma^{h}\cos(h\theta)\right)\underbrace{\left(\begin{matrix}-i&i\\
1&1\end{matrix}\right)}_{:=\boldsymbol{C}_{2}}=\left(h(\gamma e^{i\theta})^{h},h(\gamma e^{-i\theta})^{h}\right).

Let
\boldsymbol{P}_{1}=\diag(\boldsymbol{I}_{r},\boldsymbol{C}_{1},\dots,\boldsymbol{C}_{1},\boldsymbol{I}_{r},\boldsymbol{C}_{2},\dots,\boldsymbol{C}_{2})
be a
J\times J
block diagonal matrix consisting of two identity matrices
\boldsymbol{I}_{r}
and
s
repeated blocks of
\boldsymbol{C}_{1}
and
\boldsymbol{C}_{2}
.
We then have
\det(\boldsymbol{P}_{1})=(-2i)^{2s}=4^{s}
, and

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{L}_{[1:J]}(\boldsymbol{\omega})\boldsymbol{P}_{1}=\left(\begin{matrix}x_{1}&x_{2}&\cdots&x_{r+2s}&x_{1}&x_{2}&\cdots&x_{r+2s}\\
x_{1}^{2}&x_{2}^{2}&\cdots&x_{r+2s}^{2}&2x_{1}^{2}&2x_{2}^{2}&\cdots&2x_{r+2s}^{2}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
x_{1}^{J}&x_{2}^{J}&\cdots&x_{r+2s}^{J}&Jx_{1}^{J}&Jx_{2}^{J}&\cdots&Jx_{r+2s}^{J}\end{matrix}\right):=\boldsymbol{P}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{J\times J},

where
x_{j}=\lambda_{j}
for
1\leq j\leq r
, while
x_{r+2m-1}=\gamma_{m}e^{i\theta_{m}}
and
x_{r+2m}=\gamma_{m}e^{-i\theta_{m}}
for
1\leq m\leq s
, and
i
is the imaginary unit.

We subtract the
h
th column of
\boldsymbol{P}_{2}
from its
(r+2s+h)
th column, for all
1\leq h\leq r+2s
, and obtain a matrix with the same determinant as
\boldsymbol{P}_{2}
as follows,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{P}_{3}=\left(\begin{matrix}x_{1}&x_{2}&\cdots&x_{r+2s}&0&0&\cdots&0\\
x_{1}^{2}&x_{2}^{2}&\cdots&x_{r+2s}^{2}&x_{1}^{2}&x_{2}^{2}&\cdots&x_{r+2s}^{2}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
x_{1}^{J}&x_{2}^{J}&\cdots&x_{r+2s}^{J}&(J-1)x_{1}^{J}&(J-1)x_{2}^{J}&\cdots&(J-1)x_{r+2s}^{J}\end{matrix}\right).

Note that
\boldsymbol{P}_{3}=\boldsymbol{P}_{4}\boldsymbol{P}_{5}
, where

\boldsymbol{P}_{4}=\left(\begin{matrix}1&1&\cdots&1&0&0&\cdots&0\\
x_{1}&x_{2}&\cdots&x_{r+2s}&x_{1}&x_{2}&\cdots&x_{r+2s}\\
x_{1}^{2}&x_{2}^{2}&\cdots&x_{r+2s}^{2}&2x_{1}^{2}&2x_{2}^{2}&\cdots&2x_{r+2s}^{2}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
x_{1}^{J-1}&x_{2}^{J-1}&\cdots&x_{r+2s}^{J-1}&(J-1)x_{1}^{J-1}&(J-1)x_{2}^{J-1}&\cdots&(J-1)x_{r+2s}^{J-1}\end{matrix}\right)

is a generalized Vandermonde matrix
(
li2008special
)
, and
\boldsymbol{P}_{5}=\diag\{x_{1},\dots,x_{r+2s},x_{1},\dots,x_{r+2s}\}
. By
li2008special
,

|\det(\boldsymbol{P}_{4})|=\prod_{i=1}^{r+2s}x_{i}\prod_{1\leq k<h\leq r+2s}(x_{h}-x_{k})^{4}
.
As a result,

|\det(\boldsymbol{P}_{2})|=|\det(\boldsymbol{P}_{3})|=|\det(\boldsymbol{P}_{4})||\det(\boldsymbol{P}_{5})|=\prod_{h=1}^{r+2s}|x_{h}|^{3}\prod_{1\leq h<k\leq r+2s}(x_{h}-x_{k})^{4}\geq\nu_{\mathrm{lower}}^{3J/2}\nu_{\mathrm{gap}}^{J(J/2-1)}.

It follows that

\displaystyle|\det(\boldsymbol{L}_{[1:J]}(\boldsymbol{\omega}))|=\frac{|\det(\boldsymbol{P}_{2})|}{|\det(\boldsymbol{P}_{1})|}\geq 0.25^{s}\nu_{\mathrm{lower}}^{3J/2}\nu_{\mathrm{gap}}^{J(J/2-1)}>0,

(S3)

and hence
\boldsymbol{L}_{[1:J]}(\boldsymbol{\omega})
is full-rank. Moreover, combining (
S2
) and (
S3
), we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{\min}(\boldsymbol{L}_{[1:J]}(\boldsymbol{\omega}))\geq\frac{|\det(\boldsymbol{L}_{[1:J]}(\boldsymbol{\omega}))|}{\sigma_{\max}^{J-1}(\boldsymbol{L}_{[1:J]}(\boldsymbol{\omega}))}\geq\frac{0.25^{s}\nu_{\mathrm{lower}}^{3J/2}\nu_{\mathrm{gap}}^{J(J/2-1)}}{C_{\bar{\rho}}^{J-1}}=c_{\bar{\rho}}>0.

(S4)

Finally, similar to (
S1
), by the Courant–Fischer theorem, it can be shown that

\displaystyle\sigma_{\min}(\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}(\boldsymbol{\omega}))\geq\min\left\{1,\sigma_{\min}(\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega}))\right\}\geq\min\left\{1,\sigma_{\min}(\boldsymbol{L}_{[1:J]}(\boldsymbol{\omega}))\right\},

which, together with (
S4
), leads to a lower bound of
\sigma_{\min}(\boldsymbol{L}_{\rm{stack}}(\boldsymbol{\omega}))
.
In view of the aforementioned lower bound and the upper bound in (
S1
), the inequalities in the lemma are verified.
Lastly, when
r
and
s
are bounded from above, we immediately have
C_{\bar{\rho}}\asymp 1
and
c_{\bar{\rho}}\asymp 1
. The proof of this lemma is complete.

S8.3
Proof of Lemma
S4
(Deviation bound)

Since
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{h}=\boldsymbol{\widehat{G}}_{h}-\boldsymbol{G}_{h}^{*}=\boldsymbol{\widehat{D}}_{h}
for
1\leq h\leq p
, we have

\frac{1}{T}\left|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}\rangle\right|\leq\frac{1}{T}\left|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\sum_{h=1}^{p}\boldsymbol{\widehat{D}}_{h}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h}\rangle\right|+\frac{1}{T}\left|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\sum_{h=p+1}^{\infty}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{h}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h}\rangle\right|,

(S5)

where the first term on the right-hand side is suppressed if
p=0
. Without loss of generality, we assume that
p\geq 1
in what follows.
First, it can be verified that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\left|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\sum_{h=1}^{p}\boldsymbol{\widehat{D}}_{h}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h}\rangle\right|=\frac{1}{T}\left|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\widehat{\boldsymbol{D}}_{{\mathrm{AR}}}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}^{p}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\Big\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}(\boldsymbol{x}_{t}^{p})^{\top},\widehat{\boldsymbol{D}}_{{\mathrm{AR}}}\Big\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{d}}_{{\mathrm{AR}}}\|_{1}\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}(\boldsymbol{x}_{t}^{p})^{\top}\right\|_{\max},

(S6)

where
\boldsymbol{x}_{t}^{p}=(\boldsymbol{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\dots,\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p}^{\top})^{\top}
.
For the second term on the right-hand side of (
S5
), since

\displaystyle\sum_{h=p+1}^{\infty}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{h}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h}

\displaystyle=\left[\widehat{\boldsymbol{G}}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\{\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\omega}})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{G}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}^{*}\{\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}^{\top}\right]\boldsymbol{x}_{t-p}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\boldsymbol{D}}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\{\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\omega}})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}^{\top}\boldsymbol{x}_{t-p}+\boldsymbol{G}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}^{*}\left[\left\{\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\omega}})-\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\right\}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\right]^{\top}\boldsymbol{x}_{t-p},

we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\left|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\sum_{h=p+1}^{\infty}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{h}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\left|\Big\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\boldsymbol{x}_{t-p}^{\top}\{\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\omega}})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\},\widehat{\boldsymbol{D}}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\Big\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\left|\Big\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\boldsymbol{x}_{t-p}^{\top}\left[\left\{\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\omega}})-\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\right\}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\right],\boldsymbol{G}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}^{*}\Big\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{d}}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\|_{1}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\omega}\in\boldsymbol{\Omega}}\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\boldsymbol{x}_{t-p}^{\top}\{\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}\right\|_{\max}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\|\boldsymbol{g}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}^{*}\|_{1}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1}}\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\boldsymbol{x}_{t-p}^{\top}\left[\left\{\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*}+\boldsymbol{\phi})-\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\right\}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\right]\right\|_{\max},

(S7)

where we use the property that
\boldsymbol{\widehat{\phi}}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1}
.

To prove this lemma, it suffices to establish the following intermediate results:

(i)

With probability at least
1-4e^{-2\log(Np)}
,

\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}(\boldsymbol{x}_{t}^{p})^{\top}\right\|_{\max}\leq C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\log(Np)}{T}},

(S8)

where
C_{1}>0
is an absolute constant.

(ii)

With probability at least
1-5e^{-4\log N}
,

\sup_{\boldsymbol{\omega}\in\boldsymbol{\Omega}}\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\boldsymbol{x}_{t-p}^{\top}\{\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}\right\|_{\max}\leq C_{2}\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\log N}{T}}

(S9)

and

\sup_{\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1}}\frac{\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\boldsymbol{x}_{t-p}^{\top}\left[\left\{\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*}+\boldsymbol{\phi})-\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\right\}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\right]\right\|_{\max}}{T\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}}\leq C_{3}\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\log N}{T}},

(S10)

where
C_{2},C_{3}>0
are absolute constants.

Proof of (
S8
):
Note that

\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}(\boldsymbol{x}_{t}^{p})^{\top}\right\|_{\max}=\max_{1\leq i,j\leq N,1\leq k\leq p}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{i,t}y_{j,t-k}\right|.

We begin by considering any fixed triplet
(i,j,k)
such that
1\leq i,j\leq N
and
1\leq k\leq p
.
Let
\boldsymbol{\iota}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
be the
i
th unit vector, which consists of all zeros except that the
i
th entry is one. Applying Lemma
S16
with
T_{0}=-k
,
T_{1}=T
,
\boldsymbol{w}_{t}=\boldsymbol{y}_{t}
, and
\boldsymbol{M}=\boldsymbol{\iota}_{j}^{\top}
, together with Lemma
S18
(i), we have

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}y_{j,t-k}^{2}-\mathbb{E}(y_{j,t-k}^{2})\right|\geq\eta\sigma^{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})\right\}\leq 2e^{-c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}\min(\eta,\eta^{2})T},

for any
\eta>0
. In addition, by Lemma
S18
(i),
\mathbb{E}(y_{j,t-k}^{2})=\boldsymbol{\iota}_{j}^{\top}\mathbb{E}(\boldsymbol{y}_{t-k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-k}^{\top})\boldsymbol{\iota}_{j}\leq\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})=\kappa_{2}
.
Thus, by taking
\eta=(2\sigma^{2})^{-1}
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}y_{j,t-k}^{2}\geq 1.5\kappa_{2}\right)\leq 2e^{-cT},

(S11)

where
c=c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}\min\{(2\sigma^{2})^{-1},(2\sigma^{2})^{-2}\}
. Then we can show that for any
K>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{i,t}y_{j,t-k}\right|\geq K\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{i,t}y_{j,t-k}\right|\geq KT,\;\sum_{t=1}^{T}y_{j,t-k}^{2}\leq 1.5\kappa_{2}T\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}y_{j,t-k}^{2}\geq 1.5\kappa_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq 2e^{-K^{2}T/\{3\sigma^{2}\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\}}+2e^{-cT},

(S12)

where we applied Lemma
S17
(i) with
a=KT
and
b=1.5\kappa_{2}T
in the last inequality.
As a result, by applying (
S8.3
) with

K=\sqrt{\frac{6\sigma^{2}\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\log(N^{2}p)}{T}},

if
T\geq 2c^{-1}\log(N^{2}p)
, then it can be verified that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{1\leq i,j\leq N,1\leq k\leq p}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{i,t}y_{j,t-k}\right|\geq\sqrt{\frac{6\sigma^{2}\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\log(N^{2}p)}{T}}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq N^{2}p\max_{1\leq i,j\leq N,1\leq k\leq p}\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{i,t}y_{j,t-k}\right|\geq\sqrt{\frac{6\sigma^{2}\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\log(N^{2}p)}{T}}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq 2e^{-\log(N^{2}p)}+2e^{-cT+\log(N^{2}p)}\leq 4e^{-\log(N^{2}p)}.

(S13)

Hence, (
S8
) proved.

Proof of (
S9
):

Note that by Assumption
1
(i), for all
\boldsymbol{\omega}\in\boldsymbol{\Omega}
, we have
0<|\ell_{h,k}(\boldsymbol{\omega})|\leq\bar{\rho}^{h-p}
if
h\geq p+1
and
p+1\leq k\leq d
. Then we can show that

\displaystyle\sup_{\boldsymbol{\omega}\in\boldsymbol{\Omega}}\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\boldsymbol{x}_{t-p}^{\top}\{\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}\right\|_{\max}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\sup_{\boldsymbol{\omega}\in\boldsymbol{\Omega}}\max_{1\leq i,j\leq N,p+1\leq k\leq d}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{i,t}\sum_{h=p+1}^{\infty}\ell_{h,k}(\boldsymbol{\omega})y_{j,t-h}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\sup_{\boldsymbol{\omega}\in\boldsymbol{\Omega}}\max_{1\leq i,j\leq N,p+1\leq k\leq d}\left|\sum_{h=p+1}^{\infty}\ell_{h,k}(\boldsymbol{\omega})\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{i,t}y_{j,t-h}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\sum_{h=p+1}^{\infty}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\omega}\in\boldsymbol{\Omega}}\max_{p+1\leq k\leq d}|\ell_{h,k}(\boldsymbol{\omega})|\max_{1\leq i,j\leq N}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{i,t}y_{j,t-h}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\sum_{h=p+1}^{\infty}\bar{\rho}^{h-p}\max_{1\leq i,j\leq N}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{i,t}y_{j,t-h}\right|.

(S14)

To establish an upper bound for the weighted infinite sum in (
S8.3
), we first consider a fixed triplet
(i,j,h)
such that
1\leq i,j\leq N
and
h\geq p+1
. By the same arguments as those for (
S11
) except that we take
\eta=h-p
, we can show that

\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}y_{j,t-h}^{2}\geq\{(h-p)\sigma^{2}+1\}\kappa_{2}\right\}\leq 2e^{-c(h-p)T}.

(S15)

Similar to (
S8.3
), for any
K>0
, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{i,t}y_{j,t-h}\right|\geq K\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{i,t}y_{j,t-h}\right|\geq KT,\;\sum_{t=1}^{T}y_{j,t-h}^{2}\leq\{(h-p)\sigma^{2}+1\}\kappa_{2}T\right]+\mathbb{P}\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}y_{j,t-h}^{2}\geq\{(h-p)\sigma^{2}+1\}\kappa_{2}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq 2e^{-K^{2}T/[2\{(h-p)\sigma^{2}+1\}\sigma^{2}\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})]}+2e^{-c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}(h-p)T}.

Applying the above result with

K=\sqrt{\frac{4\{(h-p)\sigma^{2}+1\}(h-p+1)\sigma^{2}\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\log(N^{2})}{T}},

if
T\geq 4c^{-1}\log(N^{2})
, similar to (
S8.3
), for any fixed
h\geq p+1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\max_{1\leq i,j\leq N}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{i,t}y_{j,t-h}\right|\geq\sqrt{\frac{4\{(h-p)\sigma^{2}+1\}(h-p+1)\sigma^{2}\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\log(N^{2})}{T}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq N^{2}\max_{1\leq i,j\leq N}\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{i,t}y_{j,t-h}\right|\geq\sqrt{\frac{4\{(h-p)\sigma^{2}+1\}(h-p+1)\sigma^{2}\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\log(N^{2})}{T}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq 2e^{-2(h-p+1)\log(N^{2})+\log(N^{2})}+2e^{-c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}(h-p)T+\log(N^{2})}\leq 4e^{-2(h-p)\log(N^{2})}.

Note that
\{(h-p)\sigma^{2}+1\}(h-p+1)\sigma^{2}\leq\{2(h-p)\sigma^{2}+1\}^{2}
. Thus,

\mathbb{P}\left[\max_{1\leq i,j\leq N}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{i,t}y_{j,t-h}\right|\geq\{2(h-p)\sigma^{2}+1\}\sqrt{\frac{4\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\log(N^{2})}{T}}\right]\leq 4e^{-2(h-p)\log(N^{2})},

which can be further strengthened to a union bound for all
h\geq p+1
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\forall h\geq p+1:\max_{1\leq i,j\leq N}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{i,t}y_{j,t-h}\right|\geq\{2(h-p)\sigma^{2}+1\}\sqrt{\frac{4\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\log(N^{2})}{T}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\sum_{h=p+1}^{\infty}2e^{-2(h-p)\log(N^{2})}\leq 5e^{-4\log N},

(S16)

where the last inequality holds as long as
N\geq 2
.
Combining (
S8.3
) with (
S8.3
), we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\boldsymbol{\omega}\in\boldsymbol{\Omega}}\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\boldsymbol{x}_{t-p}^{\top}\{\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}\right\|_{\max}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h=p+1}^{\infty}\bar{\rho}^{h-p}\{2(h-p)\sigma^{2}+1\}\sqrt{\frac{4\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\log(N^{2})}{T}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\log N}{T}},

with probability at least
1-5e^{-4\log N}
. Thus, (
S9
) is proved.

Proof of (
S10
):

For any
h\geq 1
and
1\leq k\leq r
, by the Taylor expansion, we have

\ell_{h}^{I}(\lambda_{k})-\ell_{h}^{I}(\lambda_{k}^{*})=\nabla\ell_{h}^{I}(\lambda_{k}^{*})(\lambda_{k}-\lambda_{k}^{*})+\frac{1}{2}\nabla^{2}\ell_{h}^{I}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{k})(\lambda_{k}-\lambda_{k}^{*})^{2},

where
\widetilde{\lambda}_{k}
lies between
\lambda_{k}^{*}
and
\lambda_{k}
. Then, by Lemma
S2
, for any
\boldsymbol{\omega}=\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*}+\boldsymbol{\phi}
with
\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1}
,

\max_{1\leq k\leq r}|\ell_{h}^{I}(\lambda_{k})-\ell_{h}^{I}(\lambda_{k}^{*})|\leq C_{\ell}\bar{\rho}^{h}\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}+\frac{1}{2}C_{\ell}\bar{\rho}^{h}\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2C_{\ell}\bar{\rho}^{h}\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2},\quad\forall h\geq 1,

where we used the fact that
\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}\leq c_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\leq 2
for all
\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1}
. By a similar argument, for any
\boldsymbol{\omega}=\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*}+\boldsymbol{\phi}
with
\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1}
, we can show that

\max_{1\leq k\leq s,\iota=1,2}|\ell_{h}^{II,\iota}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{k})-\ell_{h}^{II,\iota}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{k}^{*})|\leq 2C_{\ell}\bar{\rho}^{h}\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2},\quad\forall h\geq 1.

As a result,

\sup_{\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1}}\max_{p+1\leq k\leq d}\frac{|\ell_{h,k}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*}+\boldsymbol{\phi})-\ell_{h,k}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})|}{\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}}\leq 2C_{\ell}\bar{\rho}^{h-p},\quad\forall h\geq p+1.

Then it follows that

\displaystyle\sup_{\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1}}\frac{\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\boldsymbol{x}_{t-p}^{\top}\left[\left\{\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*}+\boldsymbol{\phi})-\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\right\}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\right]\right\|_{\max}}{T\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\sup_{\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1}}\max_{1\leq i,j\leq N,p+1\leq k\leq d}\frac{\left|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{i,t}\sum_{h=p+1}^{\infty}\{\ell_{h,k}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*}+\boldsymbol{\phi})-\ell_{h,k}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\}y_{j,t-h}\right|}{T\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\sum_{h=p+1}^{\infty}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1}}\max_{p+1\leq k\leq d}\frac{|\ell_{h,k}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*}+\boldsymbol{\phi})-\ell_{h,k}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})|}{\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}}\max_{1\leq i,j\leq N}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{i,t}y_{j,t-h}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq 2C_{\ell}\sum_{h=p+1}^{\infty}\bar{\rho}^{h-p}\max_{1\leq i,j\leq N}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{i,t}y_{j,t-h}\right|,

which is similar to (
S8.3
). Similar to the method for (
S9
), we accomplish the proof of (
S10
) by combining the above result with (
S8.3
).

Lastly, in view of (
S5
)–(
S10
), and the fact that
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{d}}_{{\mathrm{AR}}}\|_{1}+\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{d}}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\|_{1}=\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{d}}\|_{1}
, we accomplish the proof of this lemma by taking
C_{{\mathrm{dev}}}=\max_{1\leq i\leq 3}C_{i}>0
and combining the tail probabilities for (
S8
)–(
S10
).

S8.4
Proof of Lemma
S5
(Restricted strong convexity)

By the proof of Proposition
2
, we can write

\boldsymbol{\Delta}=\boldsymbol{D}\{\boldsymbol{L}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\{\boldsymbol{P}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}^{\top}+(\boldsymbol{0}_{N\times Np},\boldsymbol{R}),

where the remainder term
\boldsymbol{R}
depends on both
\boldsymbol{\phi}
and
\boldsymbol{D}
; see (
S5.3
) and (
S13
) for details.

Let
\boldsymbol{Q}(\boldsymbol{\phi})=\left(q_{h,j}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\right)
and
\boldsymbol{S}(\boldsymbol{\phi})=\left(s_{h,j}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\right)
be
\infty\times(r+2s)
matrices whose entries are

\displaystyle q_{h,j}(\boldsymbol{\phi})

\displaystyle=\nabla\ell_{h}^{I}(\lambda_{j}^{*})(\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{j}^{*})+\frac{1}{2}\nabla^{2}\ell_{h}^{I}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{j})(\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{j}^{*})^{2},

\displaystyle s_{h,j}(\boldsymbol{\phi})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\nabla^{2}\ell_{h}^{I}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{j})(\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{j}^{*})^{2},

\displaystyle q_{h,r+2(m-1)+\iota}(\boldsymbol{\phi})

\displaystyle=(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{m}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{m}^{*})^{\top}\nabla\ell_{h}^{II,\iota}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{m}^{*})+\frac{1}{2}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{m}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{m}^{*})^{\prime}\nabla^{2}\ell_{h}^{II,\iota}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{m})(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{m}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{m}^{*}),

\displaystyle s_{h,r+2(m-1)+\iota}(\boldsymbol{\phi})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{m}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{m}^{*})^{\prime}\nabla^{2}\ell_{h}^{II,\iota}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{m})(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{m}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{m}^{*}),

where
h\geq 1
,
1\leq j\leq r
,
1\leq m\leq s
,
\iota=1,2
, and
\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}
’s and
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{m}
’s are defined as in (
S5.3
); that is,
\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}
lies between
\lambda_{j}^{*}
and
\lambda_{j}
for
1\leq j\leq r
, and
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{m}
lies between
\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}_{m}
and
\boldsymbol{\eta}_{m}
for
1\leq m\leq s
, and we suppress their dependence on
h
for notational simplicity.
Then, by the definition of
\boldsymbol{R}_{h}
’s in (
S5.3
), we can write

\boldsymbol{R}=\boldsymbol{D}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\{\boldsymbol{Q}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{G}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}^{*}\{\boldsymbol{S}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}^{\top}.

Denote

\displaystyle\begin{split}\boldsymbol{Z}&=(\boldsymbol{z}_{1},\dots,\boldsymbol{z}_{T}),\quad\boldsymbol{z}_{t}=\left\{\boldsymbol{L}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\right\}^{\top}\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\\
\boldsymbol{V}&=(\boldsymbol{v}_{1},\dots,\boldsymbol{v}_{T}),\quad\boldsymbol{v}_{t}=\left\{\boldsymbol{P}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\right\}^{\top}\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\\
\boldsymbol{H}(\boldsymbol{\phi})&=(\boldsymbol{h}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\phi}),\dots,\boldsymbol{h}_{T}(\boldsymbol{\phi})),\quad\boldsymbol{h}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\phi})=\left\{\boldsymbol{Q}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\right\}^{\top}\boldsymbol{x}_{t-p},\\
\boldsymbol{B}(\boldsymbol{\phi})&=(\boldsymbol{b}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\phi}),\dots,\boldsymbol{b}_{T}(\boldsymbol{\phi})),\quad\boldsymbol{b}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\phi})=\left\{\boldsymbol{S}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\right\}^{\top}\boldsymbol{x}_{t-p},\end{split}

(S17)

and
\boldsymbol{X}=(\boldsymbol{x}_{1},\dots,\boldsymbol{x}_{T})
. Combining all results above, we have

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Delta}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}

\displaystyle=\left[\boldsymbol{D}\{\boldsymbol{L}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\{\boldsymbol{P}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}^{\top}\right]\boldsymbol{x}_{t}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\left[\boldsymbol{D}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\{\boldsymbol{Q}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{G}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}^{*}\{\boldsymbol{S}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}^{\top}\right]\boldsymbol{x}_{t-p}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{D}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}+\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\boldsymbol{v}_{t}+\boldsymbol{D}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\boldsymbol{h}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\phi})+\boldsymbol{G}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}^{*}\boldsymbol{b}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\phi}),

or equivalently,

\boldsymbol{\Delta}\boldsymbol{X}=\boldsymbol{D}\boldsymbol{Z}+\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\boldsymbol{V}+\boldsymbol{D}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\boldsymbol{H}(\boldsymbol{\phi})+\boldsymbol{G}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}^{*}\boldsymbol{B}(\boldsymbol{\phi}).

By the triangle inequality and the fact that
(|x|+|y|)/2\leq\sqrt{x^{2}+y^{2}}
for any
x,y\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\boldsymbol{X}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\geq 0.5\|\boldsymbol{D}\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}+0.5\|\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\boldsymbol{V}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}-\|\boldsymbol{D}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\boldsymbol{H}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}-\|\boldsymbol{G}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}^{*}\boldsymbol{B}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}.

(S18)

We need to lower bound the first term and upper bound the other three terms on the right-hand side of (
S18
). We state the following intermediate results for deriving these bounds and relegate their proofs to the end of this subsection:

(i)

If

T\geq 4c_{1}^{-1}(r+2s)^{2}(\kappa_{2}/\widetilde{\kappa}_{1})^{2}\log(Nd)
, with probability at least
1-2e^{-0.5c_{1}\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}^{2}T/\{(r+2s)^{2}\kappa_{2}^{2}\}}
,

\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\boldsymbol{D}\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\geq\frac{\sqrt{\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}}}{2}\|\boldsymbol{d}\|_{2}-\sqrt{\frac{(r+2s)^{2}\kappa_{2}^{2}\log(Nd)}{c_{1}\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}T}}\|\boldsymbol{d}\|_{1},\quad\forall\boldsymbol{d}\in\mathbb{R}^{N^{2}d},

where
c_{1}>0
is an absolute constant, and
\boldsymbol{d}=\vect(\boldsymbol{D})
.

(ii)

If

T\geq 2c_{2}^{-1}(r+2s)^{3}(\kappa_{2}/\widetilde{\kappa}_{1})^{2}\max\left\{\log(12u_{\phi}^{3}/l_{\phi}^{3})+0.5\log(3\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}/\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}),\log(6u_{\phi}/l_{\phi})\right\}
, with probability at least
1-2e^{-0.5c_{2}\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}^{2}T/\{(r+2s)^{2}\kappa_{2}^{2}\}}
,

\frac{\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}l_{\phi}^{2}}{8u_{\phi}^{4}}\leq\inf_{\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}}\frac{\|\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\boldsymbol{V}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}}{T\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}^{2}}\leq\sup_{\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}}\frac{\|\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\boldsymbol{V}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}}{T\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}^{2}}\leq\frac{6\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}u_{\phi}^{2}}{l_{\phi}^{4}},

where
c_{2}>0
is an absolute constant,
l_{\phi}=(\sqrt{2}\overline{\alpha}_{\mathrm{MA}})^{-1}\min_{1\leq k\leq s}\gamma_{k}^{*}
, and
u_{\phi}=\underline{\alpha}_{\mathrm{MA}}^{-1}
.

(iii)

If
T\geq 4c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}^{-1}\log\{N(r+2s)\}
, then with probability at least
1-4e^{-0.5c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}T}
,

\sup_{\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1}}\frac{\|\boldsymbol{D}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\boldsymbol{H}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}}{T\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}^{2}}\leq C_{4}(r+2s)\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}\left[\|\boldsymbol{d}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{4\log\{N(r+2s)\}}{c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}T}\|\boldsymbol{d}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\|_{1}^{2}\right],\quad\forall\boldsymbol{d}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{N^{2}(r+2s)},

where
c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}>0
is defined as in Lemma
S19
, and
C_{4}>0
is an absolute constant.

(iv)

If
T\geq 2c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}^{-1}\log N
, then with probability at least
1-4e^{-0.5c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}T}
,

\sup_{\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1}}\frac{\|\boldsymbol{G}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}^{*}\boldsymbol{B}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}}{T\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}^{4}}\leq C_{4}\overline{\alpha}_{\mathrm{MA}}^{2}(r+2s)^{2}\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}.

Now we prove this lemma based on the above results. First note that
\boldsymbol{\Delta}=\boldsymbol{\Delta}(\boldsymbol{\phi},\boldsymbol{d})
is linear in
\boldsymbol{d}
for any fixed
\boldsymbol{\phi}
. That is, for any
\alpha\neq 0
, it holds

\alpha\boldsymbol{\Delta}(\boldsymbol{\phi},\boldsymbol{d})=(\alpha\boldsymbol{D}+\alpha\boldsymbol{G}^{*})\{\boldsymbol{L}(\boldsymbol{\phi}+\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}^{\top}-\alpha\boldsymbol{G}^{*}\{\boldsymbol{L}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}^{\top}=\boldsymbol{\Delta}(\boldsymbol{\phi},\alpha\boldsymbol{d}),

where we suppress the dependence of
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
on
\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*}
and
\boldsymbol{g}^{*}
(or
\alpha\boldsymbol{g}^{*}
) since they are fixed. As a result, it suffices to show that the conclusion stated in this lemma holds uniformly over the intersection of
\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}
and
\pazocal{S}(\delta)
with high probability, where
\pazocal{S}(\delta)=\{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times\infty}\mid\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}=\delta\}
is a sphere, for some radius
\delta>0
such that
\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)
is nonempty. The reason is that the same conclusion will remain true if we multiply
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
by any
\alpha\neq 0
.

We restrict our attention to
\boldsymbol{\Delta}=\boldsymbol{\Delta}(\boldsymbol{\phi},\boldsymbol{d})\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)
with the radius
\delta\in(0,c_{\Delta}c_{\boldsymbol{\omega}})
, where
c_{\Delta}>0
is defined as in (
S17
) in the proof of Proposition
2
. The specific
\delta
will be chosen later.
Note that by (
S17
), for a sufficiently small
\delta
, if
\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}=\delta
, then

\delta C_{\Delta}^{-1}\leq\|\boldsymbol{d}\|_{2}\leq\delta c_{\Delta}^{-1}\quad\text{and}\quad\delta C_{\Delta}^{-1}\overline{\alpha}_{\mathrm{MA}}^{-1}\leq\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}\leq\delta c_{\Delta}^{-1}\underline{\alpha}_{\mathrm{MA}}^{-1}\leq c_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}.

(S19)

The second inequality in (
S19
) indicates that
\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)\neq\emptyset
.

Note that
0<\kappa_{2}\leq\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}
. Combining the high probability events in claims (i)–(iv) with (
S18
) and (
S19
), we have the following result that holds uniformly for all
\boldsymbol{\Delta}=\boldsymbol{\Delta}(\boldsymbol{\phi},\boldsymbol{d})\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)
:

\displaystyle\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\boldsymbol{X}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}}{\sqrt{T}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\left\{\frac{\sqrt{\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}}}{2}\|\boldsymbol{d}\|_{2}-\sqrt{\frac{(r+2s)^{2}\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}^{2}\log(Nd)}{c_{1}\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}T}}\|\boldsymbol{d}\|_{1}+\sqrt{\frac{\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}l_{\phi}^{2}}{8u_{\phi}^{4}}}\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-\sqrt{C_{4}(r+2s)\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}}\left[\|\boldsymbol{d}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\|_{2}+\sqrt{\frac{4\log\{N(r+2s)\}}{c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}T}}\|\boldsymbol{d}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\|_{1}+\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{\mathrm{MA}}^{2}(r+2s)}\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}\right]\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{C_{\Delta}^{-1}\left(2+\overline{\alpha}_{\mathrm{MA}}^{-1}\sqrt{2l_{\phi}^{2}/u_{\phi}^{4}}\right)}{8}\sqrt{\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}}\cdot\delta-c_{\Delta}^{-2}\sqrt{C_{4}\left\{1+(\overline{\alpha}_{\mathrm{MA}}/\underline{\alpha}_{\mathrm{MA}})^{2}(r+2s)\right\}(r+2s)\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}}\cdot\delta^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-\left(\sqrt{\frac{(r+2s)\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}}{c\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}}}+\sqrt{\frac{4C_{4}C_{\Delta}^{-2}}{c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}}}\cdot\delta\right)\sqrt{\frac{(r+2s)\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}\log(Nd)}{T}}\|\boldsymbol{d}\|_{1},

where we used the fact that
\sqrt{x^{2}+y^{2}}\leq|x|+|y|
in the first inequality. Since
\overline{\alpha}_{\mathrm{MA}}^{-1}\sqrt{2l_{\phi}^{2}/u_{\phi}^{4}}=(\underline{\alpha}_{\mathrm{MA}}/\overline{\alpha}_{\mathrm{MA}})^{2}\min_{1\leq k\leq s}\gamma_{k}^{*}\leq\bar{\rho}<1
, by choosing

0<\delta\leq\min\left[\frac{3C_{\Delta}^{-1}\sqrt{\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}/\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}}}{16c_{\Delta}^{-2}\sqrt{C_{4}\left\{1+(\overline{\alpha}_{\mathrm{MA}}/\underline{\alpha}_{\mathrm{MA}})^{2}(r+2s)\right\}}},\;\sqrt{\frac{c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}(r+2s)\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}/\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}}{16C_{4}C_{\Delta}^{-2}c}},\;c_{\Delta}\underline{\alpha}_{\mathrm{MA}}c_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\right]

in the above inequality, then for all
\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\delta)
it holds uniformly that

\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\boldsymbol{X}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\geq\frac{3\sqrt{\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}}}{16C_{\Delta}}\cdot\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}-\sqrt{\frac{(r+2s)^{2}\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}^{2}\log(Nd)}{c\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}T}}\cdot\|\boldsymbol{d}\|_{1}.

(S20)

As mentioned earlier, for any
\alpha\neq 0
, we have
\alpha\boldsymbol{\Delta}(\boldsymbol{\phi},\boldsymbol{d})=\boldsymbol{\Delta}(\boldsymbol{\phi},\alpha\boldsymbol{d})
and hence

\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|(\alpha\boldsymbol{\Delta})\boldsymbol{X}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\geq\frac{3\sqrt{\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}}}{16C_{\Delta}}\cdot\|\alpha\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}-\sqrt{\frac{(r+2s)^{2}\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}^{2}\log(Nd)}{c\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}T}}\cdot\|\alpha\boldsymbol{d}\|_{1}.

This shows that (
S20
) will remain true uniformly for all
\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}\cap\pazocal{S}(\alpha\delta)
with any
\alpha\neq 0
, and hence (
S20
) holds for all
\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}
.

Note that for any
x,y,z\geq 0
, if
x\geq y-z
, then
y^{2}\leq(x+z)^{2}\leq 2(x^{2}+z^{2})
and hence
x^{2}\geq y^{2}/2-z^{2}
. As a result,
(
S20
) implies that

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{1}{T}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\boldsymbol{X}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\geq C\left\{\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}-\frac{(r+2s)^{2}\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}^{2}\log\{N(p\vee 1)\}}{\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}T}\|\boldsymbol{d}\|_{1}^{2}\right\}.

Finally, note that
\widetilde{\kappa}_{i}\asymp\kappa_{i}
for
i=1,2
, and
r+2s\lesssim 1
. Combining all tails probabilities and conditions on
T
from claims (i)–(iv), we accomplish the proof of this lemma.

Below we give the proofs of claims (i)–(iv).

Proof of (i):
Note that

\frac{1}{T}\|\boldsymbol{D}\boldsymbol{Z}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}=\frac{1}{T}\trace(\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{D}\boldsymbol{Z})=\trace\left(\boldsymbol{D}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{z}\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\right)=\vect(\boldsymbol{D}^{\top})^{\top}(\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\otimes\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{z})\vect(\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}),

where
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{z}=\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}/T=T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}^{\top}
.
Then, the result of this lemma can be rewritten as

|\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\otimes\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{z})\boldsymbol{u}|^{1/2}\geq\frac{\sqrt{\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}}}{2}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}-\sqrt{\frac{(r+2s)^{2}\kappa_{2}^{2}\log(Nd)}{c_{1}\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}T}}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{1},\quad\forall\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{N^{2}d},

(S21)

with probability at least
1-2e^{-0.5c_{1}\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}^{2}T/\{(r+2s)^{2}\kappa_{2}^{2}\}}
.

Let
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{z}=\mathbb{E}(\boldsymbol{z}_{t}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}^{\top})
. In addition, let
\underline{\boldsymbol{z}}_{T}=(\boldsymbol{z}_{T}^{\top},\dots,\boldsymbol{z}_{1}^{\top})^{\top}
, and denote its covariance matrix by

\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{z}=\mathbb{E}(\underline{\boldsymbol{z}}_{T}\underline{\boldsymbol{z}}_{T}^{\top})=\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{z}(j-i)\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq T},

where
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{z}(\ell)=\mathbb{E}(\boldsymbol{z}_{t}\boldsymbol{z}_{t-\ell}^{\top})
is the lag-
\ell
autocovariance matrix of
\boldsymbol{z}_{t}
for
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
, and
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{z}(0)=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{z}
.
We will first prove the following intermediate result:

\left|\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\{\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\otimes(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{z}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{z})\}\boldsymbol{u}\right|\leq\frac{\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}}{4}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{(r+2s)^{2}\kappa_{2}^{2}\log(Nd)}{c_{1}\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}T}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{1}^{2},\quad\forall\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{N^{2}d},

(S22)

with probability at least
1-2e^{-0.5c_{1}\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}^{2}T/\{(r+2s)^{2}\kappa_{2}^{2}\}}
.

Denote
\boldsymbol{U}=\boldsymbol{L}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}
, and let
\boldsymbol{\ell}_{h}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})
be the
h
th row of
\boldsymbol{L}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})
for
h\geq 1
. Then

\boldsymbol{z}_{t}=\boldsymbol{U}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}=\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{U}_{h}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h}
and
\boldsymbol{U}=(\boldsymbol{U}_{1},\boldsymbol{U}_{2},\dots)
,
where
\boldsymbol{U}_{h}=\boldsymbol{\ell}_{h}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}
for
h\geq 1
. By the definition of
\boldsymbol{L}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})
, we have

\|\boldsymbol{\ell}_{h}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\|_{2}=1
for
1\leq h\leq p
and
\|\boldsymbol{\ell}_{h}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{r+2s}\bar{\rho}^{h}
for
h\geq p+1
,
which implies

\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\|\boldsymbol{U}_{h}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}=\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\|\boldsymbol{\ell}_{h}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{r+2s}\bar{\rho}(1-\bar{\rho})^{-1}.

In addition, we have

\sigma_{\min}(\boldsymbol{U})\geq\sigma_{\min,L}.

Consequently, applying Lemma
S18
(ii) with
\boldsymbol{w}_{t}=\boldsymbol{z}_{t}
, we can show that

\lambda_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{z})\geq\kappa_{1}\sigma_{\min}^{2}(\boldsymbol{U})\geq\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}

(S23)

and

\lambda_{\max}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{z})\leq(r+2s)\bar{\rho}^{2}(1-\bar{\rho})^{-2}\kappa_{2}.

(S24)

Note that
T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}=\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{z}\boldsymbol{u}
and
\mathbb{E}(\|\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}\|_{2}^{2})=\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{z}\boldsymbol{u}
.
Furthermore, since
\boldsymbol{z}_{t}=\mathscr{W}(B)\boldsymbol{y}_{t}=\mathscr{W}(B)\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*}(B)\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}
is a zero-mean and stationary time series, where
\mathscr{W}(B)=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{W}_{i}B^{i}
, we can apply Lemma
S16
with
T_{0}=0
,
T_{1}=T
,
\boldsymbol{w}_{t}=\boldsymbol{z}_{t}
,
\boldsymbol{M}=\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}
, and
\eta=\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}/\{108\sigma^{2}(r+2s)\bar{\rho}^{2}(1-\bar{\rho})^{-2}\kappa_{2}\}
, in conjunction with (
S24
),
to obtain the following pointwise bound: for any
\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{Nd}
with
\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}\leq 1
,

\mathbb{P}\left\{\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{z}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{z})\boldsymbol{u}\geq\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}/108\right\}\leq 2\exp\left[-c_{1}\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}^{2}T/\{(r+2s)^{2}\kappa_{2}^{2}\}\right],

(S25)

where
c_{1}=c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}\min[\{108\sigma^{2}\bar{\rho}^{2}(1-\bar{\rho})^{-2}\}^{-1},\{108\sigma^{2}\bar{\rho}^{2}(1-\bar{\rho})^{-2}\}^{-2}]
.

Let
\pazocal{K}(2K)=\{\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{Nd}:\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}\leq 1,\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{0}\leq 2K\}
be a set of sparse vectors, where
K\geq 1
is an integer to be specified later. Then, by arguments similar to the proof of Lemma F.2 in
basu2015regularized
, we can strengthen (
S25
) to the union bound that holds for all
\boldsymbol{u}\in\pazocal{K}(2K)
as follows:

\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{\boldsymbol{u}\in\pazocal{K}(2K)}\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{z}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{z})\boldsymbol{u}\geq\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}/108\right\}\leq 2\exp\left[-c_{1}\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}^{2}T/\{(r+2s)^{2}\kappa_{2}^{2}\}+2K\log(Nd)\right],

Now we choose
K=\lceil 0.25c_{1}\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}^{2}T/\{(r+2s)^{2}\kappa_{2}^{2}\log(Nd)\}\rceil\geq 1
. Thus, applying Supplementary Lemma 12 in
LW12
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\forall\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{Nd}:|\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{z}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{z})\boldsymbol{u}|\leq\frac{\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}}{4}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{(r+2s)^{2}\kappa_{2}^{2}\log(Nd)}{c_{1}\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}T}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{1}^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\geq 1-2\exp\left[-0.5c_{1}\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}^{2}T/\{(r+2s)^{2}\kappa_{2}^{2}\}\right],

and hence (
S22
).
Furthermore, by (
S23
) and the inequality
|x+y|^{1/2}\leq|x|^{1/2}+|y|^{1/2}
, for all
\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{N^{2}d}
, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}\leq\lambda_{\min}^{1/2}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{z})\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq|\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\otimes\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{z})\boldsymbol{u}|^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq|\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\otimes\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{z})\boldsymbol{u}|^{1/2}+|\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\{\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\otimes(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{z}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{z})\}\boldsymbol{u}|^{1/2}.

Finally, combining this with (
S22
) and the inequality
\sqrt{x^{2}+y^{2}}\leq|x|+|y|
, we have (
S21
). This completes the proof of (i).

Proof of (ii):

It is worth noting that
\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi})
is linear in
\boldsymbol{\phi}
, which implies that

\frac{\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi})}{\|\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}=\{\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}\mid\|\boldsymbol{M}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}=1\},\quad\forall\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi},

(S26)

where
\boldsymbol{\Xi}=\left\{\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N(r+2s)}\mid\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}\right\}
. To prove the result of this lemma, we begin by establishing the following intermediate result:

\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}l_{\phi}^{2}}{8u_{\phi}^{2}}\leq\inf_{\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}}\frac{1}{T}\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{V}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\leq\sup_{\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}}\frac{1}{T}\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{V}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\leq\frac{6\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}u_{\phi}^{2}}{l_{\phi}^{2}}\right)\geq 1-2e^{-0.5c_{2}\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}^{2}T/\{(r+2s)^{2}\kappa_{2}^{2}\}}.

(S27)

Similar to the proof of claim (i), let
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{v}=\mathbb{E}(\boldsymbol{v}_{t}\boldsymbol{v}_{t}^{\top})
. In addition, let
\underline{\boldsymbol{v}}_{T}=(\boldsymbol{v}_{T}^{\top},\dots,\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\top})^{\top}
, and denote its covariance matrix by

\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{v}=\mathbb{E}(\underline{\boldsymbol{v}}_{T}\underline{\boldsymbol{v}}_{T}^{\top})=\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{v}(j-i)\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq T},

where
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{v}(\ell)=\mathbb{E}(\boldsymbol{v}_{t}\boldsymbol{v}_{t-\ell}^{\top})
is the lag-
\ell
autocovariance matrix of
\boldsymbol{v}_{t}
for
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
, and
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{v}(0)=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{v}
.

Denote
\boldsymbol{U}=\boldsymbol{P}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}
and let
\boldsymbol{p}_{h}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})
be the
h
th row of
\boldsymbol{P}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})
for
h\geq 1
. Then

\boldsymbol{v}_{t}=\boldsymbol{U}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}=\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{U}_{h}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h}
and
\boldsymbol{U}=(\boldsymbol{U}_{1},\boldsymbol{U}_{2},\dots)
,
where
\boldsymbol{U}_{h}=\boldsymbol{p}_{h}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}
for
h\geq 1
. By the definition of
\boldsymbol{P}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})
, we have
\|\boldsymbol{p}_{h}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{r+2s}C_{\ell}\bar{\rho}^{h}
for
h\geq 1
,
which implies

\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\|\boldsymbol{U}_{h}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}=\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\|\boldsymbol{p}_{h}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{r+2s}C_{\ell}\bar{\rho}(1-\bar{\rho})^{-1}.

In addition, we have

\sigma_{\min,L}\leq\sigma_{\min}(\boldsymbol{U})\leq\sigma_{\max}(\boldsymbol{U})\leq\sigma_{\max,L}.

Consequently, applying Lemma
S18
(ii) with
\boldsymbol{w}_{t}=\boldsymbol{v}_{t}
, we can show that

\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}\leq\kappa_{1}\sigma_{\min}^{2}(\boldsymbol{U})\leq\lambda_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{v})\leq\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{v})\leq\kappa_{2}\sigma_{\max}^{2}(\boldsymbol{U})\leq\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}

(S28)

and

\lambda_{\max}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{v})\leq(r+2s)C_{\ell}^{2}\bar{\rho}^{2}(1-\bar{\rho})^{-2}\kappa_{2},

(S29)

Note that
T^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{V}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}=T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{v}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}=\trace(\boldsymbol{M}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{v}\boldsymbol{M}^{\top})
, where
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{v}=\boldsymbol{V}\boldsymbol{V}^{\top}/T=T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{v}_{t}\boldsymbol{v}_{t}^{\top}
, and
\mathbb{E}(\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{v}_{t}\|_{2}^{2})=\trace(\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{v}\boldsymbol{M}^{\top})
. By (
S28
), for any
\boldsymbol{M}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N(r+2s)}
, we have

\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}\|\boldsymbol{M}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\leq\lambda_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{v})\|\boldsymbol{M}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}\left(\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{v}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\leq\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{v})\|\boldsymbol{M}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\leq\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}\|\boldsymbol{M}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}.

Moreover, by Lemma
S16
with
T_{0}=0
,
T_{1}=T
,
\boldsymbol{w}_{t}=\boldsymbol{v}_{t}
, and
\eta=\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}/\{2\sigma^{2}(r+2s)C_{\ell}^{2}\bar{\rho}^{2}(1-\bar{\rho})^{-2}\kappa_{2}\}
, in conjunction with (
S29
), we can show that for any
\boldsymbol{M}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N(r+2s)}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{v}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}-\mathbb{E}\left(\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{v}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\right|\geq\frac{\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}}{2}\|\boldsymbol{M}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left[-c_{2}\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}^{2}T/\{(r+2s)^{2}\kappa_{2}^{2}\}\right].

where
c_{2}=c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}\min[\{2\sigma^{2}C_{\ell}^{2}\bar{\rho}^{2}(1-\bar{\rho})^{-2}\}^{-1},\{2\sigma^{2}C_{\ell}^{2}\bar{\rho}^{2}(1-\bar{\rho})^{-2}\}^{-2}]
. As a result, we have the following pointwise bound: for any
\boldsymbol{M}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N(r+2s)}
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}}{2}\|\boldsymbol{M}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{T}\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{V}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\leq\frac{3\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}}{2}\|\boldsymbol{M}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\right)\geq 1-2\exp\left[-c_{2}\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}^{2}T/\{(r+2s)^{2}\kappa_{2}^{2}\}\right].

(S30)

Next we strengthen the above pointwise bound to a union bound that holds for all
\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
. Let
\boldsymbol{\bar{\Xi}}(\epsilon_{0})
be a minimal generalized
\epsilon_{0}
-net of
\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
in the Frobenius norm, where
0<\epsilon_{0}<1
will be chosen later.
By Lemma
S20
(ii), any
\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\bar{\Xi}}(\epsilon_{0})
satisfies
l_{\phi}/u_{\phi}\leq\|\boldsymbol{M}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq u_{\phi}/l_{\phi}
. Define the event

\mathscr{E}(\epsilon_{0})=\left\{\forall\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{M}$}\in\boldsymbol{\bar{\Xi}}(\epsilon_{0}):\sqrt{\frac{\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}l_{\phi}^{2}}{2u_{\phi}^{2}}}<\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{V}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}<\sqrt{\frac{3\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}u_{\phi}^{2}}{2l_{\phi}^{2}}}\right\}.

Then, by the pointwise bounds in (
S30
) and the covering number in Lemma
S20
(i),
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\{{\mathscr{E}}^{\complement}(\epsilon_{0})\}

\displaystyle\leq e^{(r+2s)\log\{3/(c_{\boldsymbol{M}}\epsilon_{0})\}}\max_{\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\bar{\Xi}}(\epsilon_{0})}\mathbb{P}\left[{\left\{\frac{\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}l_{\phi}^{2}}{2u_{\phi}^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{T}\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{V}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\leq\frac{3\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}u_{\phi}^{2}}{2l_{\phi}^{2}}\right\}}^{\complement}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left[-c_{2}\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}^{2}T/\{(r+2s)^{2}\kappa_{2}^{2}\}+(r+2s)\log\{3u_{\phi}/(l_{\phi}\epsilon_{0})\}\right].

(S31)

By Lemma
S20
(iii), it holds

\mathscr{E}(\epsilon_{0})\subset\left\{\max_{\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\bar{\Xi}}(\epsilon_{0})}\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{V}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{3\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}u_{\phi}^{2}}{2l_{\phi}^{2}}}\right\}\subset\left\{\sup_{\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{V}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\frac{\sqrt{3\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}u_{\phi}^{2}/(2l_{\phi}^{2})}}{1-\epsilon_{0}}\right\}.

(S32)

Moreover, by a method similar to that for the proof of Lemma
S20
(iii), for any
\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
and its corresponding
\bar{\boldsymbol{M}}\in\boldsymbol{\bar{\Xi}}(\epsilon_{0})
defined therein, we can show that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{V}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\bar{\boldsymbol{M}}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{V}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|(\boldsymbol{M}-\bar{\boldsymbol{M}})_{(1)}\boldsymbol{V}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}

\displaystyle\geq\min_{\bar{\boldsymbol{M}}\in\boldsymbol{\bar{\Xi}}(\epsilon)}\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\bar{\boldsymbol{M}}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{V}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}-\epsilon_{0}\sup_{\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{V}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}.

Taking the infimum over all
\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
and combining the result with (
S32
), we can show that on the event
\mathscr{E}(\epsilon_{0})
, it holds

\displaystyle\inf_{\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{V}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{\frac{\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}l_{\phi}^{2}}{2u_{\phi}^{2}}}-\epsilon_{0}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{3\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}u_{\phi}^{2}/(2l_{\phi}^{2})}}{1-\epsilon_{0}}\geq\sqrt{\frac{\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}l_{\phi}^{2}}{2u_{\phi}^{2}}}-2\epsilon_{0}\sqrt{\frac{3\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}u_{\phi}^{2}}{2l_{\phi}^{2}}}

if
0<\epsilon_{0}\leq 1/2
. Thus, by setting

\epsilon_{0}=\min\left\{\frac{l_{\phi}^{2}}{4u_{\phi}^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}}{3\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}}},\frac{1}{2}\right\},

we have

\mathscr{E}(\epsilon_{0})\subset\left\{\inf_{\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{V}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\geq\frac{\sqrt{\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}l_{\phi}^{2}/(2u_{\phi}^{2})}}{2}\right\}.

(S33)

Consequently, with the above choice of
\epsilon_{0}
, we have

\mathscr{E}(\epsilon_{0})\subset\left\{\frac{\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}l_{\phi}^{2}}{8u_{\phi}^{2}}\leq\inf_{\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}}\frac{1}{T}\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{V}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\leq\sup_{\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}}\frac{1}{T}\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{V}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\leq\frac{6\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}u_{\phi}^{2}}{l_{\phi}^{2}}\right\},

which, together with (
S8.4
), implies that

\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}l_{\phi}^{2}}{8u_{\phi}^{2}}\leq\inf_{\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}}\frac{1}{T}\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{V}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\leq\sup_{\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}}\frac{1}{T}\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{V}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\leq\frac{6\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}u_{\phi}^{2}}{l_{\phi}^{2}}\right)\geq 1-2e^{-0.5c_{2}\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}^{2}T/\{(r+2s)^{2}\kappa_{2}^{2}\}}

under the condition on
T
stated in (ii). Then (
S27
) follows immediately. By combining
(
S26
), (
S27
), and the bounds in (
S14
), we accomplish the proof of (ii).

Proof of (iii):

Similar to the proof of claim (i), we can show that
