\frac{1}{T}\|\boldsymbol{D}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\boldsymbol{H}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}=\trace\left\{\boldsymbol{D}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{H}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\boldsymbol{D}_{\mathrm{MA}}^{\top}\right\}=\vect(\boldsymbol{D}_{\mathrm{MA}}^{\top})^{\top}\{\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\otimes\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{H}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\}\vect(\boldsymbol{D}_{\mathrm{MA}}^{\top}),

where
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{H}(\boldsymbol{\phi})=\boldsymbol{H}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\boldsymbol{H}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{\phi})/T=T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{h}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\boldsymbol{h}_{t}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{\phi})
. Then, the high probability event stated in this lemma is equivalent to

\sup_{\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1}}\frac{|\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\{\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\otimes\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{H}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\}\boldsymbol{u}|}{\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}^{2}}\leq C_{4}(r+2s)\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}\left[\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{4\log\{N(r+2s)\}}{c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}T}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{1}^{2}\right],\quad\forall\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{N^{2}(r+2s)}.

Thus, similar to the proof of (
S22
), it suffices to show that with probability at least
1-4e^{-0.5c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}T}
,

\sup_{\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1}}\frac{|\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{H}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\boldsymbol{u}|}{\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}^{2}}\leq C_{4}(r+2s)\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}\left[\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{4\log\{N(r+2s)\}}{c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}T}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{1}^{2}\right],\quad\forall\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{N(r+2s)}.

(S34)

To prove (
S34
), we first aim to establish an upper bound of
|\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{H}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\boldsymbol{u}|
for a fixed
\boldsymbol{u}=(\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\top},\dots,\boldsymbol{u}_{r+2s}^{\top})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{N(r+2s)}
, where
\boldsymbol{u}_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
for
1\leq k\leq r+2s
.
Note that
\boldsymbol{h}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\phi})=\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\{\boldsymbol{q}_{h}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-h}
, where
\boldsymbol{q}_{h}(\boldsymbol{\phi})=(q_{h,1}(\boldsymbol{\phi}),q_{h,2}(\boldsymbol{\phi}),\dots)^{\top}
is the transpose of the
h
th row of
\boldsymbol{Q}(\boldsymbol{\phi})
.
Then

\displaystyle|\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{H}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\boldsymbol{u}|

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\boldsymbol{h}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\boldsymbol{h}_{t}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\boldsymbol{u}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\left|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\{\boldsymbol{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-i}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-h}^{\top}\{\boldsymbol{q}_{h}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}\boldsymbol{u}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}[\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\{\boldsymbol{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-i}]^{2}\right)^{1/2}\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}[\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\{\boldsymbol{q}_{h}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-h}]^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\left\{\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}[\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\{\boldsymbol{q}_{h}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-h}]^{2}\right)^{1/2}\right\}^{2}.

In addition,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}[\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\{\boldsymbol{q}_{h}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-h}]^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{r+2s}q_{h,k}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\top}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-h}\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{r+2s}q_{h,k}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\sum_{k=1}^{r+2s}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\top}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-h})^{2}

\displaystyle=\|\boldsymbol{q}_{h}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\|_{2}^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{r+2s}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\top}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-h})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\boldsymbol{q}_{h}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\|_{2}^{2}\left\{\sum_{k=1}^{r+2s}\sqrt{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\top}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-h})^{2}}\right\}^{2}.

Furthermore, by Lemma
S2
and a method similar to that for upper bounding
\|\boldsymbol{R}_{1h}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}
and
\|\boldsymbol{R}_{2h}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}
in the proof of Proposition
2
, we can show that

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{q}_{h}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}C_{\ell}\bar{\rho}^{h}\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}+\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}C_{\ell}\bar{\rho}^{h}\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2\sqrt{2}C_{\ell}\bar{\rho}^{h}\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2},\quad\forall\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1}.

Combining the above results, we have

|\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{H}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\boldsymbol{u}|\leq\left\{2\sqrt{2}C_{\ell}\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}\sum_{k=1}^{r+2s}\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\bar{\rho}^{h}\sqrt{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\top}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-h})^{2}}\right\}^{2},\quad\forall\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1}.

Hence, by Lemma
S19
, if
T\geq c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}^{-1}\log 2
, for any fixed
\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{N(r+2s)}
, it holds with probability at least
1-4e^{-c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}T}
that

\displaystyle\sup_{\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1}}\frac{|\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{H}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\boldsymbol{u}|}{\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 8C_{\ell}^{2}\left\{\sum_{k=1}^{r+2s}\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\bar{\rho}^{h}\sqrt{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\top}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-h})^{2}}\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 8C_{\ell}^{2}(r+2s)\sum_{k=1}^{r+2s}\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\bar{\rho}^{2h}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{\top}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-h})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 8C_{\ell}^{2}(r+2s)\sum_{k=1}^{r+2s}\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\bar{\rho}^{2h}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})(h\sigma^{2}+1)\|\boldsymbol{u}_{k}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=C_{4}(r+2s)\kappa_{2}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C_{4}(r+2s)\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}^{2},

(S35)

where

0<C_{4}=8C_{\ell}^{2}\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\bar{\rho}^{2h}(h\sigma^{2}+1)<\infty
is an absolute constant.

Next we strengthen the above bound to (
S34
) by a method similar to that for (
S22
) in the proof of claim (i).
Let
\pazocal{K}(2K)=\{\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{N(r+2s)}:\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}\leq 1,\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{0}\leq 2K\}
be a set of sparse vectors, where
K\geq 1
is an integer to be specified later. Then, by arguments similar to the proof of Lemma F.2 in
basu2015regularized
, we have the union bound:

\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{\boldsymbol{u}\in\pazocal{K}(2K)}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1}}\frac{|\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{H}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\boldsymbol{u}|}{\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}^{2}}\geq C_{4}(r+2s)\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}\leq 4e^{-c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}T+2K\log\{N(r+2s)\}},

By choosing
K=\lceil 0.25c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}T/\log\{N(r+2s)\}\rceil\geq 1
and using Supplementary Lemma 12 in
LW12
, we can readily verify (
S34
) and thus accomplish the proof of (iii).

Proof of (iv):

Similar to the proof of claim (iii), we have

\frac{1}{T}\|\boldsymbol{G}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}^{*}\boldsymbol{B}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}=\trace\left\{\boldsymbol{G}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}^{*}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{b}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\boldsymbol{G}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}^{*\top}\right\}=\vect(\boldsymbol{G}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}^{*\top})^{\top}\{\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\otimes\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{b}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\}\vect(\boldsymbol{G}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}^{*\top}),

(S36)

where
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{b}(\boldsymbol{\phi})=\boldsymbol{B}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\boldsymbol{B}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{\phi})/T=T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{b}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\boldsymbol{b}_{t}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{\phi})
.
Moreover, we can establish an upper bound of
|\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{b}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\boldsymbol{u}|
for any fixed
\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{N(r+2s)}
. Note that
\boldsymbol{b}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\phi})=\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\{\boldsymbol{s}_{h}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-p-h}
, where
\boldsymbol{s}_{h}(\boldsymbol{\phi})=(s_{h,1}(\boldsymbol{\phi}),s_{h,2}(\boldsymbol{\phi}),\dots)^{\top}
is the transpose of the
h
th row of
\boldsymbol{S}(\boldsymbol{\phi})
. In addition, by Lemma
S2
and a method similar to that for upper bounding
\|\boldsymbol{R}_{3h}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}
in the proof of Proposition
2
, we can show that

\|\boldsymbol{s}_{h}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\|_{2}\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}C_{\ell}\bar{\rho}^{h}\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}^{4},\quad\forall\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1}.

Then by Lemma
S19
, along the lines of (
S8.4
) it can be readily proved that if
T\geq c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}^{-1}\log 2
, for any fixed
\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{N(r+2s)}
, with probability at least
1-4e^{-c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}T}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1}}\frac{|\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{b}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\boldsymbol{u}|}{\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}^{4}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{4}(r+2s)\kappa_{2}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C_{4}(r+2s)\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}^{2},

where
C_{4}>0
is the absolute constant defined as in (
S8.4
). For simplicity, denote
\vect(\boldsymbol{G}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}^{*\top})=(\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\top},\dots,\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{\top})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{N^{2}(r+2s)}
, where
\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{N(r+2s)}
for
1\leq i\leq N
.
Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1}}\frac{|\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\{\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\otimes\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{b}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\}\boldsymbol{u}|}{\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}^{4}}\geq C_{4}(r+2s)\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1}}\frac{|\boldsymbol{u}_{i}^{\top}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{b}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\boldsymbol{u}_{i}|}{\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}^{4}}\geq C_{4}(r+2s)\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1}}\frac{|\boldsymbol{u}_{i}^{\top}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{b}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\boldsymbol{u}_{i}|}{\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}^{4}}\geq C_{4}(r+2s)\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq 4e^{-c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}T+\log N}\leq 4e^{-c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}T/2},

if
T\geq 2c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}^{-1}\log N
.
Note that
\|\boldsymbol{G}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}^{*}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\leq(r+2s)\overline{\alpha}_{\mathrm{MA}}^{2}
.
Combining these results with (
S36
), we accomplish the proof of (iv).

S8.5
Proof of Lemma
S6
(Effect of initial values I))

Note that

S_{1}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}})=\frac{2}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\sum_{h=t}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}}_{h}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h}\rangle=\frac{2}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{3}S_{1i}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}}),

(S37)

where

\displaystyle S_{11}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}})=\sum_{t=1}^{p}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\sum_{h=t}^{p}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}}_{h}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h}\rangle=\sum_{t=1}^{p}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\sum_{h=t}^{p}\boldsymbol{\widehat{D}}_{h}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h}\rangle,

\displaystyle S_{12}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}})=\sum_{t=1}^{p}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\sum_{h=p+1}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}}_{h}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h}\rangle,\quad\text{and}\quad S_{13}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}})=\sum_{t=p+1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\sum_{h=t}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}}_{h}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h}\rangle,

with
\boldsymbol{\widehat{D}}_{h}=\boldsymbol{\widehat{G}}_{h}-\boldsymbol{G}_{h}^{*}=\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}}_{h}
for
1\leq h\leq p
. Without loss of generality, we assume that
p\geq 1
; otherwise,
S_{11}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}})
will simply disappear.

Note that

\displaystyle|S_{11}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}})|=\left|\sum_{h=1}^{p}\sum_{t=1}^{h}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\boldsymbol{\widehat{D}}_{h}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h}\rangle\right|=\left|\sum_{h=1}^{p}\langle\sum_{t=1}^{h}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h}^{\top},\boldsymbol{\widehat{D}}_{h}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h=1}^{p}\|\vect(\boldsymbol{\widehat{D}}_{h})\|_{1}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{h}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h}^{\top}\right\|_{\max}

\displaystyle\leq\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{d}}_{{\mathrm{AR}}}\|_{1}\max_{1\leq h\leq p}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{h}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h}^{\top}\right\|_{\max}.

For any fixed
1\leq h\leq p
, by a method similar to that for claim (i) in the proof of Lemma
S4
, we can show that

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{h}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h}^{\top}\right\|_{\max}\leq C_{1}\sqrt{h\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\log N}\right\}\geq 1-4e^{-2\log N}.

As a result, with probability at least
1-4pe^{-2\log N}
, we have

|S_{11}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}})|\leq C_{1}\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{d}}_{{\mathrm{AR}}}\|_{1}\sqrt{p\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\log N}.

(S38)

For
S_{12}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}})
, similar to (
S8.3
), we have

\displaystyle|S_{12}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}})|

\displaystyle\leq\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{d}}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\|_{1}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\omega}\in\boldsymbol{\Omega}}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{p}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\boldsymbol{x}_{t-p}^{\top}\{\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}\right\|_{\max}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\|\boldsymbol{g}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}^{*}\|_{1}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1}}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{p}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\boldsymbol{x}_{t-p}^{\top}\left[\left\{\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*}+\boldsymbol{\phi})-\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\right\}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\right]\right\|_{\max}

By a method similar to that for claim (ii) in the proof of Lemma
S4
, we can show that with probability at least
1-4e^{-4\log N}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\boldsymbol{\omega}\in\boldsymbol{\Omega}}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{p}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\boldsymbol{x}_{t-p}^{\top}\{\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}\right\|_{\max}\leq C_{2}\sqrt{p\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\log N},

\displaystyle\sup_{\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1}}\frac{\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{p}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\boldsymbol{x}_{t-p}^{\top}\left[\left\{\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*}+\boldsymbol{\phi})-\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\right\}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\right]\right\|_{\max}}{\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}}\leq C_{3}\sqrt{p\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\log N}.

Therefore, with probability at least
1-5e^{-4\log N}
,

|S_{12}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}})|\leq\sqrt{p}(C_{2}+C_{3})(\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{d}}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\|_{1}+\|\boldsymbol{g}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}^{*}\|_{1}\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\phi}}\|_{2})\sqrt{\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\log N},

(S39)

Now we handle
S_{13}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}})
.
For any
t\geq p+1
, let
\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}}_{[t]}=(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}}_{t},\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}}_{t+1},\dots)
be the horizontal concatenation of
\{\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}}_{h}\}_{h\geq t}
. For any
h\geq 1
, let
\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}_{[h]}(\boldsymbol{\omega})
be the matrix obtained by removing the first
h-1
rows of
\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega})
. For any
t\geq p+1
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{h=t}^{\infty}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{h}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}}_{[t]}\boldsymbol{x}_{1}=\left[\widehat{\boldsymbol{G}}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\{\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}_{[t-p]}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\omega}})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{G}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}^{*}\{\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}_{[t-p]}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}^{\top}\right]\boldsymbol{x}_{1}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\boldsymbol{D}}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\{\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}_{[t-p]}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\omega}})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}^{\top}\boldsymbol{x}_{1}+\boldsymbol{G}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}^{*}\left[\left\{\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}_{[t-p]}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\omega}})-\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}_{[t-p]}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\right\}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\right]^{\top}\boldsymbol{x}_{1}.

Thus, we can apply arguments similar to those for claim (ii) in the proof of Lemma
S4
to handle
S_{13}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}})
. First, similar to (
S8.3
), we can show that

\displaystyle|S_{13}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}})|

\displaystyle\leq\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{d}}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\|_{1}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\omega}\in\boldsymbol{\Omega}}\left\|\sum_{t=p+1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\boldsymbol{x}_{1}^{\top}\{\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}_{[t-p]}(\boldsymbol{\omega})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}\right\|_{\max}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\|\boldsymbol{g}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}^{*}\|_{1}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1}}\left\|\sum_{t=p+1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\boldsymbol{x}_{1}^{\top}\left[\left\{\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}_{[t-p]}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*}+\boldsymbol{\phi})-\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}_{[t-p]}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\right\}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\right]\right\|_{\max}.

Similar to (
S8.3
), we can show that

\displaystyle\sup_{\boldsymbol{\omega}\in\boldsymbol{\Omega}}\left\|\sum_{t=p+1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\boldsymbol{x}_{1}^{\top}\{\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}_{[t-p]}(\boldsymbol{\omega})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}\right\|_{\max}=\sup_{\boldsymbol{\omega}\in\boldsymbol{\Omega}}\max_{1\leq i,j\leq N,p+1\leq k\leq d}\left|\sum_{t=p+1}^{T}\varepsilon_{i,t}\sum_{h=t}^{\infty}\ell_{h,k}(\boldsymbol{\omega})y_{j,t-h}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\sum_{h=p+1}^{\infty}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\omega}\in\boldsymbol{\Omega}}\max_{p+1\leq k\leq d}|\ell_{h,k}(\boldsymbol{\omega})|\max_{1\leq i,j\leq N}\left|\sum_{t=p+1}^{h\wedge T}\varepsilon_{i,t}y_{j,t-h}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\sum_{h=p+1}^{\infty}\bar{\rho}^{h-p}\max_{1\leq i,j\leq N}\left|\sum_{t=p+1}^{h\wedge T}\varepsilon_{i,t}y_{j,t-h}\right|,

(S40)

and, similar to (
S8.3
), it can be verified that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\forall h\geq p+1:\max_{1\leq i,j\leq N}\left|\sum_{t=p+1}^{h\wedge T}\varepsilon_{i,t}y_{j,t-h}\right|\geq\{2(h-p)\sigma^{2}+1\}\sqrt{8(h-p)\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\log N}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq 5e^{-4\log N}.

As a result, with probability at least
1-5e^{-4\log N}
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\boldsymbol{\omega}\in\boldsymbol{\Omega}}\left\|\sum_{t=p+1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\boldsymbol{x}_{1}^{\top}\{\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}_{[t-p]}(\boldsymbol{\omega})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}\right\|_{\max}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h=p+1}^{\infty}\bar{\rho}^{h-p}\{2(h-p)\sigma^{2}+1\}\sqrt{8(h-p)\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\log N}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\log N}.

Furthermore, along the lines of (
S10
), we can simultaneously derive the upper bound:

\sup_{\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1}}\frac{\left\|\sum_{t=p+1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\boldsymbol{x}_{1}^{\top}\left[\left\{\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}_{[t-p]}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*}+\boldsymbol{\phi})-\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}_{[t-p]}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\right\}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\right]\right\|_{\max}}{\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}}\lesssim\sqrt{\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\log N}.

In view of the above results, with probability at least
1-5e^{-4\log N}
, we have

|S_{13}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}})|\leq C_{5}(\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{d}}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\|_{1}+\|\boldsymbol{g}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}^{*}\|_{1}\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\phi}}\|_{2})\sqrt{\kappa_{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\log N},

(S41)

for some absolute constant
C_{5}>0
.

Let
C_{{\mathrm{init}}1}=2(C_{1}+C_{2}+C_{3}+C_{5})
, where
C_{i}
’s are from (
S38
)–(
S41
). By (
S37
)–(
S41
) and the fact that
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{d}}_{{\mathrm{AR}}}\|_{1}+\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{d}}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\|_{1}=\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{d}}\|_{1}
, we accomplish the proof of this lemma.

S8.6
Proof of Lemma
S7
(Effect of initial values II)

Similar to the proof of Lemma
S6
, consider the partition

S_{2}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}})=\frac{2}{T}\sum_{t=2}^{T}\langle\sum_{h=t}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{A}_{h}^{*}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h},\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}}_{k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-k}\rangle=\frac{2}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{3}S_{2i}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}}),

(S42)

where

\displaystyle S_{21}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}})=\sum_{t=2}^{p+1}\langle\sum_{h=t}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{A}_{h}^{*}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h},\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}}_{k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-k}\rangle=\sum_{t=2}^{p+1}\langle\sum_{h=t}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{A}_{h}^{*}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h},\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\boldsymbol{\widehat{D}}_{k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-k}\rangle,

\displaystyle S_{22}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}})=\sum_{t=p+2}^{T}\langle\sum_{h=t}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{A}_{h}^{*}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h},\sum_{k=1}^{p}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}}_{k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-k}\rangle=\sum_{t=p+2}^{T}\langle\sum_{h=t}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{A}_{h}^{*}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h},\sum_{k=1}^{p}\boldsymbol{\widehat{D}}_{k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-k}\rangle,

\displaystyle S_{23}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}})=\sum_{t=p+2}^{T}\langle\sum_{h=t}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{A}_{h}^{*}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h},\sum_{k=p+1}^{t-1}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}}_{k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-k}\rangle,

with
\boldsymbol{\widehat{D}}_{h}=\boldsymbol{\widehat{G}}_{h}-\boldsymbol{G}_{h}^{*}=\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}}_{h}
for
1\leq h\leq p
. Without loss of generality, we assume that
p\geq 1
; otherwise,
S_{21}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}})
will simply disappear. The above partition allows us to upper bound
|S_{2i}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}})|
by arguments similar to that for
S_{1i}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}})
in the proof of Lemma
S6
, for each
1\leq i\leq 3
.

Specifically, we begin by considering
S_{21}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}})
. Note that

\displaystyle|S_{21}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}})|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{k=1}^{p}\langle\sum_{t=k+1}^{p+1}\sum_{h=t}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{A}_{h}^{*}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h},\boldsymbol{\widehat{D}}_{k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-k}\rangle\right|=\left|\sum_{k=1}^{p}\langle\sum_{h=k+1}^{\infty}\sum_{t=k+1}^{h\wedge(p+1)}\boldsymbol{A}_{h}^{*}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-k}^{\top},\boldsymbol{\widehat{D}}_{k}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{p}\|\vect(\boldsymbol{\widehat{D}}_{k})\|_{1}\left\|\sum_{h=k+1}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{A}_{h}^{*}\sum_{t=k+1}^{h\wedge(p+1)}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-k}^{\top}\right\|_{\max}

\displaystyle\leq\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{d}}_{{\mathrm{AR}}}\|_{1}\cdot\max_{1\leq k\leq p}\left\|\sum_{h=k+1}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{A}_{h}^{*}\sum_{t=k+1}^{h\wedge(p+1)}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-k}^{\top}\right\|_{\max}.

(S43)

Let
\boldsymbol{a}_{i,h}^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
denote the
i
th row vector of
\boldsymbol{A}_{h}^{*}
, for
1\leq i\leq N
and
h\geq 1
. We can show that

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq k\leq p}\left\|\sum_{h=k+1}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{A}_{h}^{*}\sum_{t=k+1}^{h\wedge(p+1)}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-k}^{\top}\right\|_{\max}

\displaystyle=\max_{1\leq k\leq p}\max_{1\leq i,j\leq N}\left|\sum_{h=k+1}^{\infty}\sum_{t=k+1}^{h\wedge(p+1)}y_{i,t-k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h}^{\top}\boldsymbol{a}_{j,h}^{*}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{1\leq k\leq p}\sum_{h=k+1}^{\infty}\max_{1\leq i,j\leq N}\left|\sum_{t=k+1}^{h\wedge(p+1)}y_{i,t-k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h}^{\top}\boldsymbol{a}_{j,h}^{*}\right|

\displaystyle=\max_{1\leq k\leq p}\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\max_{1\leq i,j\leq N}\left|\sum_{t=1}^{h\wedge(p+1-k)}y_{i,t}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h}^{\top}\boldsymbol{a}_{j,h+k}^{*}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\max_{1\leq k\leq p}\max_{1\leq i,j\leq N}\left|\sum_{t=1}^{h\wedge(p+1-k)}y_{i,t}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h}^{\top}\boldsymbol{a}_{j,h+k}^{*}\right|,

(S44)

where the second last equality follows from a change of variables. For any fixed
(i,h,k,j)
with
1\leq i,j\leq N
,
1\leq k\leq p
and
h\geq 1
, note that
h\wedge(p+1-k)\leq p
.

We first focus on the case where
h\geq p+1
.
Similar to (
S15
), we can show that

\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{p}\left|\sum_{t=1}^{h\wedge(p+1-k)}y_{i,t}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h}^{\top}\boldsymbol{a}_{j,h+k}^{*}\right|\geq\{(h-p)\sigma^{2}+1\}\kappa_{2}\|\boldsymbol{a}_{j,h+k}^{*}\|_{2}\right\}\leq 2e^{-c(h-p)T}.

By Lemma
S2
, for any
1\leq j\leq N
and
h\geq p+1
we have

\|\boldsymbol{a}_{j,h+k}^{*}\|_{2}\leq C\bar{\rho}^{h-p},

(S45)

for some absolute constant
C>0
.
As a result, if
T\geq 4c^{-1}\log(N^{2}p)
, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{1\leq k\leq p}\max_{1\leq i,j\leq N}\left|\sum_{t=1}^{h\wedge(p+1-k)}y_{i,t}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h}^{\top}\boldsymbol{a}_{j,h+k}^{*}\right|\geq C\kappa_{2}\{(h-p)\sigma^{2}+1\}p\bar{\rho}^{h-p}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq 2N^{2}pe^{-c(h-p)T}\leq 2e^{-4(h-p)\log(Np)},

which can be further strengthened to a union bound for all
h\geq p+1
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg\{\forall h\geq p+1:\max_{1\leq k\leq p}\max_{1\leq i,j\leq N}\left|\sum_{t=1}^{h\wedge(p+1-k)}y_{i,t}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h}^{\top}\boldsymbol{a}_{j,h+k}^{*}\right|\geq C\kappa_{2}\{(h-p)\sigma^{2}+1\}p\bar{\rho}^{h-p}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\sum_{h=p+1}^{\infty}2e^{-4(h-p)\log(Np)}\leq 3e^{-4\log(Np)},

where the last inequality holds as long as
N\geq 2
. In addition, for each
1\leq h\leq p
, by a similar method, we can show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{1\leq k\leq p}\max_{1\leq i,j\leq N}\left|\sum_{t=1}^{h\wedge(p+1-k)}y_{i,t}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h}^{\top}\boldsymbol{a}_{j,h+k}^{*}\right|\geq C\kappa_{2}(2\sigma^{2}+1)p\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq 2e^{-4\log(Np)},

Combining the above results with (
S8.6
) and (
S8.6
), we have with probability at least
1-(3+4p)e^{-4\log(Np)}
,

|S_{21}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}})|\lesssim p\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{d}}_{{\mathrm{AR}}}\|_{1}\kappa_{2}.

(S46)

Next, for
i=2
and 3, the upper bound for
|S_{2i}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}})|
can be readily established by combining techniques we have used above for
|S_{21}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}})|
and methods similar to those for
|S_{1i}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}})|
in the proof of Lemma
S6
. That is, for each
i=2
and 3, we can show that
with probability at least
1-Cpe^{-c\log(Np)}
,

|S_{2i}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Delta}})|\lesssim p(\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{d}}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\|_{1}+\|\boldsymbol{g}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}^{*}\|_{1}\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\phi}}\|_{2})\kappa_{2}.

(S47)

Since the proof of this result follows closely the lines of (
S39
) and (
S41
) in the proof of Lemma
S6
(with only slight modifications to exploit the decay property similar to (
S45
)), but will be rather tedious, we omit the details here.

Combining (
S42
), (
S46
), (
S47
), and the fact that
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{d}}_{{\mathrm{AR}}}\|_{1}+\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{d}}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\|_{1}=\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{d}}\|_{1}
, we accomplish the proof of this lemma.

S8.7
Proof of Lemma
S8
(Effect of initial values III)

For any
t\geq p+1
, let
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{[t]}=(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{t},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{t+1},\dots)
be the horizontal concatenation of
\{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{h}\}_{h\geq t}
. Note that

\displaystyle|S_{3}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|=\frac{3}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Big\|\sum_{k=t}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-k}\Big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{3}{T}\Bigg\{\sum_{t=1}^{p}\Big\|\sum_{k=t}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-k}\Big\|_{2}^{2}+\underbrace{\sum_{t=p+1}^{T}\Big\|\sum_{k=t}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-k}\Big\|_{2}^{2}}_{S_{33}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3}{T}\left\{2\sum_{i=1}^{2}S_{3i}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})+S_{33}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\right\},

(S48)

where

\displaystyle S_{31}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{p}\Big\|\sum_{k=t}^{p}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-k}\Big\|_{2}^{2}=\sum_{t=1}^{p}\Big\|\sum_{k=t}^{p}\boldsymbol{D}_{k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-k}\Big\|_{2}^{2},

\displaystyle S_{32}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{p}\Big\|\sum_{k=p+1}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-k}\Big\|_{2}^{2}=\sum_{t=1}^{p}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{[p+1]}\boldsymbol{x}_{t-p}\|_{2}^{2},

\displaystyle S_{33}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=p+1}^{T}\Big\|\sum_{k=t}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-k}\Big\|_{2}^{2}=\sum_{t=p+1}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{[t]}\boldsymbol{x}_{1}\|_{2}^{2},

with
\boldsymbol{D}_{h}=\boldsymbol{G}_{h}-\boldsymbol{G}_{h}^{*}=\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{h}
for
1\leq h\leq p
. Without loss of generality, we assume that
p\geq 1
; otherwise, both
S_{31}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})
and
S_{32}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})
will simply disappear.

We first consider
S_{31}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})
. For any
k\geq 1
, denote
\boldsymbol{X}_{0}^{k}=(\boldsymbol{y}_{0},\dots,\boldsymbol{y}_{1-k})
. It can be verified that

\displaystyle S_{31}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{p}\langle\sum_{k=t}^{p}\boldsymbol{D}_{k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-k},\sum_{j=t}^{p}\boldsymbol{D}_{j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-j}\rangle=\sum_{k=1}^{p}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sum_{t=1}^{k\wedge j}\langle\boldsymbol{D}_{k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-k},\boldsymbol{D}_{j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-j}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{p}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{k\wedge j}\|\boldsymbol{D}_{k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-k}\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{k\wedge j}\|\boldsymbol{D}_{j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-j}\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\sum_{k=1}^{p}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{k}\|\boldsymbol{D}_{k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-k}\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\right\}^{2}=\left(\sum_{k=1}^{p}\|\boldsymbol{D}_{k}\boldsymbol{X}_{0}^{k}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\right)^{2}.

(S49)

For each fixed
1\leq k\leq p
, we can apply techniques similar to those for the proof of claim (i) in Section
S8.4
to upper bound
\|\boldsymbol{D}_{k}\boldsymbol{X}_{0}^{k}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}
.
Specifically, note that

\frac{1}{k}\|\boldsymbol{D}_{k}\boldsymbol{X}_{0}^{k}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}=\frac{1}{k}\trace(\boldsymbol{X}_{0}^{k\top}\boldsymbol{D}_{k}^{\top}\boldsymbol{D}_{k}\boldsymbol{X}_{0}^{k})=\trace\left(\boldsymbol{D}_{k}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{y}^{k}\boldsymbol{D}_{k}^{\top}\right)=\vect(\boldsymbol{D}_{k}^{\top})^{\top}(\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\otimes\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{y}^{k})\vect(\boldsymbol{D}_{k}^{\top}),

(S50)

where
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{y}^{k}=\boldsymbol{X}_{0}^{k}\boldsymbol{X}_{0}^{k\top}/k=k^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-k}^{\top}
. Similar to (
S25
), by applying Lemmas
S16
(ii) and
S18
, where we take
T_{0}=0
,
T_{1}=k
,
\boldsymbol{w}_{t}=\boldsymbol{y}_{t-k}
,
\boldsymbol{M}=\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}
, and
\eta=\log N/(108\sigma^{2})
, we can derive
the following pointwise bound: for any
\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
with
\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}\leq 1
,

\mathbb{P}\left\{\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{y}^{k}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{y})\boldsymbol{u}\geq\kappa_{2}\log N/108\right\}\leq 2e^{-ck\log N},

where
c=c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}\min\{(108\sigma^{2})^{-1},(108\sigma^{2})^{-2}\}
. Let
\pazocal{K}(2K)=\{\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}:\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}\leq 1,\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{0}\leq 2K\}
be a set of sparse vectors, where
K\geq 1
is an integer to be specified later. Then, by arguments similar to the proof of Lemma F.2 in
basu2015regularized
, we can strengthen the above pointwise bound to the union bound as follows:

\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{\boldsymbol{u}\in\pazocal{K}(2K)}\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{y}^{k}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{y})\boldsymbol{u}\geq\kappa_{2}\log N/108\right\}\leq 2e^{-ck\log N+2K\log N},

Now we choose
K=\lceil 0.25ck\log N\rceil
. Consequently, by Supplementary Lemma 12 in
LW12
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left\{\forall\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}:|\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{y}^{k}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{y})\boldsymbol{u}|\leq\frac{\kappa_{2}\log N}{4}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\kappa_{2}}{ck}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{1}^{2}\right\}\geq 1-2e^{-0.5ck\log N}.

This further implies that

\mathbb{P}\left\{\forall\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{N^{2}}:|\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\{\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\otimes(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{y}^{k}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{y})\}\boldsymbol{u}|\leq\frac{\kappa_{2}\log N}{4}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\kappa_{2}}{ck}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{1}^{2}\right\}\geq 1-2e^{-0.5ck\log N}.

Furthermore, by Lemma
S18
, we have
|\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\otimes\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{y})\boldsymbol{u}|\leq\kappa_{2}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2\kappa_{2}\log N\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}^{2}
if
N\geq 2
. As a result, for any
1\leq k\leq p
, we have

\displaystyle|\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\otimes\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{y}^{k})\boldsymbol{u}|

\displaystyle\leq|\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\otimes\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{y})\boldsymbol{u}|+|\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\{\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\otimes(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{y}^{k}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{y})\}\boldsymbol{u}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{9\kappa_{2}\log N}{4}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\kappa_{2}}{c}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{1}^{2},\quad\forall\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{N^{2}},

with probability at least
1-2e^{-0.5c\log N}
. Then, applying the inequality
|x+y|^{1/2}\leq|x|^{1/2}+|y|^{1/2}
, from the above result we further have

\mathbb{P}\left\{\forall\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{N^{2}}:|\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\otimes\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{y}^{k})\boldsymbol{u}|^{1/2}\leq\sqrt{\frac{9\kappa_{2}\log N}{4}}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{2}}{c}}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{1}\right\}\geq 1-2e^{-0.5c\log N}.

Thus, in view of (
S50
), for any
1\leq k\leq p
, letting
\boldsymbol{u}=\vect(\boldsymbol{D}_{k}^{\top})^{\top}
, we have

\frac{\|\boldsymbol{D}_{k}\boldsymbol{X}_{0}^{k}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}}{\sqrt{k}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{9\kappa_{2}\log N}{4}}\|\boldsymbol{D}_{k}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}+\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{2}}{c}}\|\boldsymbol{D}_{k}\|_{1},\quad\forall\boldsymbol{D}_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N},

(S51)

with probability at least
1-2e^{-0.5c\log N}
. This, together with (
S8.7
), implies that

\displaystyle S_{31}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})

\displaystyle\leq p\left(\sqrt{\frac{9\kappa_{2}\log N}{4}}\sum_{k=1}^{p}\|\boldsymbol{D}_{k}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}+\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{2}}{c}}\sum_{k=1}^{p}\|\boldsymbol{D}_{k}\|_{1}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\kappa_{2}p\log N)\|\boldsymbol{d}_{{\mathrm{AR}}}\|_{2}^{2}+\kappa_{2}p\|\boldsymbol{d}_{{\mathrm{AR}}}\|_{1}^{2},\quad\forall\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon},

(S52)

with probability at least
1-2e^{-0.5c\log N}
.

Next we consider
S_{32}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})
. The method will be similar to that for Lemma
S5
. Specifically, by (
S5.3
) and (
S13
), we can show that

\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{[p+1]}=\boldsymbol{D}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\{\boldsymbol{L}^{\mathrm{MA}}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\{\boldsymbol{P}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{D}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\{\boldsymbol{Q}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{G}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}^{*}\{\boldsymbol{S}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}^{\top},

where
\boldsymbol{P}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*}),\boldsymbol{Q}(\boldsymbol{\phi}),\boldsymbol{S}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\in\mathbb{R}^{\infty\times(r+2s)}
and

\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N(r+2s)}
are defined as in the proof of Lemma
S5
. For simplicity, with a slight modification to the notation in (
S17
), we define

\displaystyle\begin{split}\boldsymbol{Z}_{-p}&=(\boldsymbol{z}_{1-p},\dots,\boldsymbol{z}_{0}),\quad\boldsymbol{z}_{t}=\left\{\boldsymbol{L}^{\mathrm{MA}}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\right\}^{\top}\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\\
\boldsymbol{V}_{-p}&=(\boldsymbol{v}_{1-p},\dots,\boldsymbol{v}_{0}),\quad\boldsymbol{v}_{t}=\left\{\boldsymbol{P}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\right\}^{\top}\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\\
\boldsymbol{H}_{-p}(\boldsymbol{\phi})&=(\boldsymbol{h}_{1-p}(\boldsymbol{\phi}),\dots,\boldsymbol{h}_{0}(\boldsymbol{\phi})),\quad\boldsymbol{h}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\phi})=\left\{\boldsymbol{Q}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\right\}^{\top}\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\\
\boldsymbol{B}_{-p}(\boldsymbol{\phi})&=(\boldsymbol{b}_{1-p}(\boldsymbol{\phi}),\dots,\boldsymbol{b}_{0}(\boldsymbol{\phi})),\quad\boldsymbol{b}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\phi})=\left\{\boldsymbol{S}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\right\}^{\top}\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\end{split}

(S53)

and
\boldsymbol{X}_{-p}=(\boldsymbol{x}_{1-p},\dots,\boldsymbol{x}_{0})
.
Consequently,

\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{[p+1]}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}=\boldsymbol{D}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\boldsymbol{z}_{t}+\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\boldsymbol{v}_{t}+\boldsymbol{D}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\boldsymbol{h}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\phi})+\boldsymbol{G}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}^{*}\boldsymbol{b}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\phi}),

and then

\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{[p+1]}\boldsymbol{X}_{-p}=\boldsymbol{D}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\boldsymbol{Z}_{-p}+\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\boldsymbol{V}_{-p}+\boldsymbol{D}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\boldsymbol{H}_{-p}(\boldsymbol{\phi})+\boldsymbol{G}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}^{*}\boldsymbol{B}_{-p}(\boldsymbol{\phi}).

Moreover, by the triangle inequality,

\displaystyle S_{32}^{1/2}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})

\displaystyle=\left\{\sum_{t=1}^{p}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{[p+1]}\boldsymbol{x}_{t-p}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}^{1/2}=\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{[p+1]}\boldsymbol{X}_{-p}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\boldsymbol{D}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\boldsymbol{Z}_{-p}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}+\|\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\boldsymbol{V}_{-p}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}+\|\boldsymbol{D}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\boldsymbol{H}_{-p}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}+\|\boldsymbol{G}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}^{*}\boldsymbol{B}_{-p}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}.

(S54)

Now our task is to upper bound each of the four terms on the right-hand side of (
S8.7
). It is worth noting the resemblance of the above terms to those in (
S18
). In fact, although claim (i) in the proof of Lemma
S5
focuses on the lower bound, similar techniques can be used to derive an upper bound for
\|\boldsymbol{D}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\boldsymbol{Z}_{-p}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}
; see also the arguments that lead to (
S51
) above. Specifically, we can show that

\frac{\|\boldsymbol{D}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\boldsymbol{Z}_{-p}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}}{\sqrt{p}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{9(r+2s)\kappa_{2}\log(Np)}{4}}\|\boldsymbol{d}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\|_{2}+\sqrt{\frac{(r+2s)\kappa_{2}}{c}}\|\boldsymbol{d}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\|_{1},\quad\forall\boldsymbol{d}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{N^{2}p},

(S55)

with probability at least
1-2e^{-0.5c\log(Np)}
.

Furthermore, by arguments similar to those for (
S30
), we have for any
\boldsymbol{M}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N(r+2s)}
the pointwise bound:

\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{V}_{-p}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}}{\sqrt{p}}\leq\sqrt{\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}\{1+\log(Np)\}}\|\boldsymbol{M}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\right)\geq 1-2e^{-2c\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}^{2}p\log(Np)/\{(r+2s)\kappa_{2}\}^{2}}.

To strengthen it to a union bound that holds for all
\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
, consider a minimal generalized
1/2
-net
\boldsymbol{\bar{\Xi}}(1/2)
of
\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
in the Frobenius norm.
By Lemma
S20
(ii), any
\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\bar{\Xi}}(1/2)
satisfies
\|\boldsymbol{M}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq u_{\phi}/l_{\phi}
.
Then, by the discretization and covering number in Lemma
S20
, we can show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\sup_{\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}}\frac{\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{V}_{-p}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}}{\sqrt{p}}\geq 2(u_{\phi}/l_{\phi})\sqrt{\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}\{1+\log(Np)\}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\max_{\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\bar{\Xi}}(1/2)}\frac{\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{V}_{-p}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}}{\sqrt{p}}\geq(u_{\phi}/l_{\phi})\sqrt{\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}\{1+\log(Np)\}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq e^{(r+2s)\log(6/c_{\boldsymbol{M}})}\max_{\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\bar{\Xi}}(1/2)}\mathbb{P}\left[\frac{\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{V}_{-p}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}}{\sqrt{p}}\geq(u_{\phi}/l_{\phi})\sqrt{\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}\{1+\log(Np)\}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left[-2c\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}^{2}p\log(Np)/\{(r+2s)\kappa_{2}\}^{2}+(r+2s)\log(6u_{\phi}/l_{\phi})\right].

Combining this with
(
S26
) and the upper bound in (
S14
),
under the condition that
\log(Np)\geq c^{-1}(r+2s)^{2}(\kappa_{2}/\widetilde{\kappa}_{1})^{2}\log(6u_{\phi}/l_{\phi})
, we have

\sup_{\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}}\frac{\|\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\boldsymbol{V}_{-p}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}}{\sqrt{p}\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}}\leq(u_{\phi}/l_{\phi}^{2})\sqrt{\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}\{1+\log(Np)\}},

(S56)

with probability at least
1-2e^{-c\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}^{2}p\log(Np)/\{(r+2s)\kappa_{2}\}^{2}}
.

We can also derive upper bounds for the third and last terms in (
S8.7
) by slightly modifying the proofs of claims (iii) and (iv) in the proof of Lemma
S5
, respectively. Denote
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{h}^{p}(\boldsymbol{\phi})=\boldsymbol{H}_{-p}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\boldsymbol{H}_{-p}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{\phi})/p=p^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{p}\boldsymbol{h}_{t-p}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\boldsymbol{h}_{t-p}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{\phi})
. Along the lines of (
S8.4
) we can show that for any fixed
\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{N(r+2s)}
, if
p\log\{N(r+2s)\}\geq\max\{1,c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}^{-1}\log 2\}
, then with probability at least
1-4e^{-c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}p\log\{N(r+2s)\}}
,

\sup_{\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1}}\frac{|\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{h}^{p}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\boldsymbol{u}|}{\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}^{2}}\leq C_{4}\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}^{2}\log\{N(r+2s)\},

where
C_{4}>0
is the absolute constant defined as in the proof of Lemma
S5
. Note that, however, a bit different from (
S8.4
), the above result is obtained by taking
\eta=\log\{N(r+2s)\}
when applying Lemma
S19
. Then, by a method similar to that for (
S34
) but taking the sparsity level
K=\lceil 0.25c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}p\log\{N(r+2s)\}\rceil
, we can show that with probability at least
1-4e^{-0.5c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}p\log\{N(r+2s)\}}
,

\sup_{\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1}}\frac{|\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{h}^{p}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\boldsymbol{u}|}{\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}^{2}}\leq C_{4}\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}\left[\log\{N(r+2s)\}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{4}{c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}p}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{1}^{2}\right],\quad\forall\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{N(r+2s)}.

Thus, analogous to the result of claim (iii) in the proof of Lemma
S5
, it then follows that

\sup_{\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1}}\frac{\|\boldsymbol{D}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\boldsymbol{H}_{-p}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}}{p\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}^{2}}\leq C_{4}\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}\left[\log\{N(r+2s)\}\|\boldsymbol{d}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{4}{c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}p}\|\boldsymbol{d}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\|_{1}^{2}\right],\quad\forall\boldsymbol{d}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{N^{2}(r+2s)},

(S57)

with probability at least
1-4e^{-0.5c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}p\log\{N(r+2s)\}}
. In addition, we can derive an upper bound for the last term in (
S8.7
) by a slight modification to the proof of claim (iv) in Section
S8.4
in the same spirit as above. The key is to apply Lemma
S19
with
\eta=(2\log N)/(c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}p)
. It can be readily verified that if
2\log N\geq c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}p
, then

\sup_{\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1}}\frac{\|\boldsymbol{G}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}^{*}\boldsymbol{B}_{-p}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}}{p\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}^{4}}\leq C_{4}\overline{\alpha}_{\mathrm{MA}}^{2}(r+2s)\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}\cdot\frac{2\log N}{c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}p},

(S58)

with probability at least
1-4e^{-\log N}
. Therefore, in view of (
S8.7
)–(
S58
), by a method similar to that for the proof of Lemma
S5
, we can show that

S_{32}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\lesssim\{\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}(r+2s)p\log\{N(p\vee 1)\}\}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}p\|\boldsymbol{d}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\|_{1}^{2},\quad\forall\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon},

(S59)

with probability at least
1-2e^{-0.5c\log(Np)}-2e^{-c(\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}/\widetilde{\kappa}_{2})^{2}p\log(Np)}-4e^{-0.5c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}p\log\{N(r+2s)\}}-4e^{-\log N}=1-Ce^{-c(\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}/\widetilde{\kappa}_{2})^{2}p\log\{N(p\vee 1)\}}
.

Lastly, we derive an upper bound for
S_{33}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})
. In fact, the method will be very similar to that for
S_{32}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})
. For any
h\geq 1
, let
\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}_{[h]}(\boldsymbol{\omega})
be the matrix obtained by removing the first
h-1
rows of
\boldsymbol{L}^{{\mathrm{MA}}}(\boldsymbol{\omega})
. Similarly, let

\boldsymbol{P}_{[h]}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*}),\boldsymbol{Q}_{[h]}(\boldsymbol{\phi})
, and
\boldsymbol{S}_{[h]}(\boldsymbol{\phi})
be the matrices obtained by removing the first
h-1
rows of

\boldsymbol{P}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*}),\boldsymbol{Q}(\boldsymbol{\phi})
, and
\boldsymbol{S}(\boldsymbol{\phi})
, respectively. Then for any
t\geq p+1
, we have

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{[t]}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{D}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\{\boldsymbol{L}^{\mathrm{MA}}_{[t-p]}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\{\boldsymbol{P}_{[t-p]}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}^{\top}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\boldsymbol{D}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\{\boldsymbol{Q}_{[t-p]}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{G}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}^{*}\{\boldsymbol{S}_{[t-p]}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\}^{\top}.

As a result, we can show that

\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{[t]}\boldsymbol{x}_{1}=\boldsymbol{D}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{z}}_{t}+\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\boldsymbol{\widetilde{v}}_{t}+\boldsymbol{D}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{h}}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\phi})+\boldsymbol{G}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}^{*}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{b}}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\phi}),

and further

\displaystyle S_{33}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=p+1}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{[t]}\boldsymbol{x}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 4\sum_{t=p+1}^{T}\left\{\|\boldsymbol{D}_{\mathrm{MA}}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{z}}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\boldsymbol{\widetilde{v}}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{D}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{h}}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\|_{2}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{G}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}^{*}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{b}}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\|_{2}^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=4\left\{\|\boldsymbol{D}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{Z}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\boldsymbol{\widetilde{V}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{D}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{H}}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{G}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}^{*}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{B}}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\right\},

(S60)

where

\displaystyle\begin{split}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{Z}}&=(\boldsymbol{\widetilde{z}}_{p+1},\dots,\boldsymbol{\widetilde{z}}_{T}),\quad\boldsymbol{\widetilde{z}}_{t}=\left\{\boldsymbol{L}^{\mathrm{MA}}_{[t-p]}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\right\}^{\top}\boldsymbol{x}_{1},\\
\boldsymbol{\widetilde{V}}&=(\boldsymbol{\widetilde{v}}_{p+1},\dots,\boldsymbol{\widetilde{v}}_{T}),\quad\boldsymbol{\widetilde{v}}_{t}=\left\{\boldsymbol{P}_{[t-p]}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\right\}^{\top}\boldsymbol{x}_{1},\\
\boldsymbol{\widetilde{H}}(\boldsymbol{\phi})&=(\boldsymbol{\widetilde{h}}_{p+1}(\boldsymbol{\phi}),\dots,\boldsymbol{\widetilde{h}}_{T}(\boldsymbol{\phi})),\quad\boldsymbol{\widetilde{h}}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\phi})=\left\{\boldsymbol{Q}_{[t-p]}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\right\}^{\top}\boldsymbol{x}_{1},\\
\boldsymbol{\widetilde{B}}(\boldsymbol{\phi})&=(\boldsymbol{\widetilde{b}}_{p+1}(\boldsymbol{\phi}),\dots,\boldsymbol{\widetilde{b}}_{T}(\boldsymbol{\phi})),\quad\boldsymbol{\widetilde{b}}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\phi})=\left\{\boldsymbol{S}_{[t-p]}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\right\}^{\top}\boldsymbol{x}_{1}.\end{split}

It then remains to derive upper bounds for each of the four summands in (
S8.7
). Despite the resemblance of the above to (
S8.7
), it is important to recognize that
\boldsymbol{\widetilde{z}}_{t},\boldsymbol{\widetilde{v}}_{t},\boldsymbol{\widetilde{h}}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\phi})
and
\boldsymbol{\widetilde{b}}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\phi})
are not stationary, unlike
\boldsymbol{z}_{t},\boldsymbol{v}_{t},\boldsymbol{h}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\phi})
and
\boldsymbol{b}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\phi})
. Indeed, the key to establishing upper bounds for the terms in (
S8.7
) is to exploit the property that the magnitude of these variables diminishes exponentially fast as
t
increases. For succinctness, we will demonstrate the key trick using
\|\boldsymbol{D}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{Z}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}
as an example. The other three summands in (
S8.7
) can be handled by using the same trick in conjunction with methods for upper bounding the analogous terms in (
S8.7
).

Note that by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle\left\{\sum_{t=p+1}^{h}\Big\|\sum_{k=p+1}^{d}\ell_{h,k}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\boldsymbol{D}_{k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h}\Big\|_{2}^{2}\right\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{r+2s}\left\{\sum_{t=p+1}^{h}\sum_{k=p+1}^{d}|\ell_{h,k}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})|^{2}\|\boldsymbol{D}_{k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\bar{\rho}^{h-p}\sqrt{r+2s}\left\{\sum_{k=p+1}^{d}\sum_{t=p+1}^{h}\|\boldsymbol{D}_{k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\bar{\rho}^{h-p}\sqrt{r+2s}\sum_{k=p+1}^{d}\left\{\sum_{t=p+1}^{h}\|\boldsymbol{D}_{k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\bar{\rho}^{h-p}\sqrt{r+2s}\sum_{k=p+1}^{d}\|\boldsymbol{D}_{k}\boldsymbol{X}_{0}^{h-p}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}},

where
\boldsymbol{X}_{0}^{h-p}=(\boldsymbol{y}_{p+1-h},\dots,\boldsymbol{y}_{0})
. This leads to

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{D}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{Z}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=p+1}^{T}\Big\|\sum_{k=p+1}^{d}\boldsymbol{D}_{k}\sum_{h=t}^{\infty}\ell_{h,k}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h}\Big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=p+1}^{T}\Big\langle\sum_{h=t}^{\infty}\sum_{k=p+1}^{d}\ell_{h,k}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\boldsymbol{D}_{k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h},\sum_{h=t}^{\infty}\sum_{i=p+1}^{d}\ell_{h,i}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\boldsymbol{D}_{i}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-i}\Big\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=p+1}^{\infty}\sum_{h=p+1}^{\infty}\sum_{t=p+1}^{h\wedge h\wedge T}\Big\langle\sum_{k=p+1}^{d}\ell_{h,k}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\boldsymbol{D}_{k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h},\sum_{i=p+1}^{d}\ell_{h,i}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\boldsymbol{D}_{i}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-i}\Big\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\left[\sum_{h=p+1}^{\infty}\left\{\sum_{t=p+1}^{h\wedge T}\Big\|\sum_{k=p+1}^{d}\ell_{h,k}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{*})\boldsymbol{D}_{k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-h}\Big\|_{2}^{2}\right\}^{1/2}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq(r+2s)\left[\sum_{k=p+1}^{d}\sum_{h=p+1}^{\infty}\bar{\rho}^{h-p}\|\boldsymbol{D}_{k}\boldsymbol{X}_{0}^{h-p}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\right]^{2}.

By Lemma
S19
and a method similar to that for (
S51
), for any fixed
p+1\leq k\leq d
, we can show that

\frac{\|\boldsymbol{D}_{k}\boldsymbol{X}_{0}^{h-p}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}}{\sqrt{h-p}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{9\kappa_{2}(h-p)\log N}{4}}\|\boldsymbol{D}_{k}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}+\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{2}(h-p)}{c}}\|\boldsymbol{D}_{k}\|_{1},\quad\forall\boldsymbol{D}_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N},\forall h\geq p+1

with probability at least
1-4e^{-0.5c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}\log N}
. As a result, we have

\|\boldsymbol{D}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{Z}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\lesssim(r+2s)\left\{(\kappa_{2}\log N)\|\boldsymbol{d}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\|_{2}^{2}+\kappa_{2}\|\boldsymbol{d}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\|_{1}^{2}\right\},\quad\forall\boldsymbol{d}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{N^{2}(r+2s)},

with probability at least
1-4(r+2s)e^{-0.5c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}\log N}
. Along the same lines, we can establish upper bounds for the other three summands in (
S8.7
) and obtain

S_{33}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\lesssim(r+2s)\left\{(\kappa_{2}\log N)\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}+\kappa_{2}\|\boldsymbol{d}_{{\mathrm{MA}}}\|_{1}^{2}\right\},\quad\forall\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon},

(S61)

with probability at least
1-C(r+2s)e^{-c(\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}/\widetilde{\kappa}_{2})^{2}p\log\{N(p\vee 1)\}}
.

Finally, note that
\widetilde{\kappa}_{i}\asymp\kappa_{i}
for
i=1,2
. Thus, combining (
S8.7
), (
S8.7
), (
S59
) and (
S61
), we have

|S_{3}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|\leq\frac{C_{{\mathrm{init}}3}\kappa_{2}(r+2s)}{T}\left(\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\log N+\|\boldsymbol{d}\|_{1}^{2}\right),\quad\forall\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon},

with probability at least
1-C(r+2s)e^{-c(\kappa_{1}/\kappa_{2})^{2}p\log\{N(p\vee 1)\}}
. Since
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\in\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}
, the proof is complete.

S8.8
Additional lemmas for proofs of Lemmas
S4
–
S8

This section contains several lemmas used to establish Lemmas
S4
–
S8
. Their proofs are given in Section
S8.9
.

Firstly, in Lemmas
S16
–
S18
below, we adopt the following notations. Let
\{\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\}
be a generic time series taking values in
\mathbb{R}^{M}
,
where
M
is an arbitrary positive integer. If
\{\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\}
is stationary with mean zero, then we denote the covariance matrix of
\boldsymbol{w}_{t}
by

\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{w}=\mathbb{E}(\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}^{\top})
.
In addition, let
\underline{\boldsymbol{w}}_{T}=(\boldsymbol{w}_{T}^{\top},\dots,\boldsymbol{w}_{1}^{\top})^{\top}
, and denote its covariance matrix by

\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{w}=\mathbb{E}(\underline{\boldsymbol{w}}_{T}\underline{\boldsymbol{w}}_{T}^{\top})=\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{w}(j-i)\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq T},

where
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{w}(\ell)=\mathbb{E}(\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\boldsymbol{w}_{t-\ell}^{\top})
is the lag-
\ell
autocovariance matrix of
\boldsymbol{w}_{t}
for
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
, and
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{w}(0)=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{w}
.
For a particular time series
\{\boldsymbol{y}_{t}\}
, accordingly we define
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{y}=\mathbb{E}(\boldsymbol{y}_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{t}^{\top})
and
\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{y}=\mathbb{E}(\underline{\boldsymbol{y}}_{T}\underline{\boldsymbol{y}}_{T}^{\top})=\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{y}(j-i)\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq T}
, where
\underline{\boldsymbol{y}}_{T}=(\boldsymbol{y}_{T}^{\top},\dots,\boldsymbol{y}_{1}^{\top})^{\top}
,
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{y}(\ell)=\mathbb{E}(\boldsymbol{y}_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-\ell}^{\top})
is the lag-
\ell
covariance matrix of
\boldsymbol{y}_{t}
for
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
, and
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{y}=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{y}(0)
.

Lemma S16

(Hanson-Wright inequalities for stationary time series)
.

Suppose that Assumption
3
holds for
\{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\}
, and
\{\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\}
is a time series with the VMA(
\infty
) representation,

\boldsymbol{w}_{t}=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{j}^{w}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t-j},

where

\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{j}^{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{M\times N}
for all
j
, and
\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\|\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{j}^{w}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}<\infty
. Let
T_{0}
be a fixed integer, and let
T_{1}
be a fixed positive integer.
Then, for any
\boldsymbol{M}\in\mathbb{R}^{Q\times M}
with
Q\geq 1
and any
\eta>0
, it holds

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|\frac{1}{T_{1}}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T_{0}+T_{1}}\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}-\mathbb{E}\left(\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\right|\geq\eta\sigma^{2}\lambda_{\max}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{w})\|\boldsymbol{M}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}\right\}\leq 2e^{-c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}\min(\eta,\eta^{2})T_{1}}.

Lemma S17

(Martingale concentration inequality)
.

Suppose that Assumption
3
holds for
\{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\}
. Let

\mathscr{F}_{t}=\sigma\{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t-1},\dots\}
for
t\in\mathbb{Z}
be a filtration. Let
\{\boldsymbol{y}_{t}\}
be a zero-mean time series, where
\boldsymbol{y}_{t}=(y_{1,t},\dots,y_{N,t})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
is
\mathscr{F}_{t-1}
-measurable.
Let
T_{0}
be a fixed integer, and let
T_{1}
be a fixed positive integer.
Fix
1\leq i,j\leq N
and
k\geq 1
. For any
a,b>0
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T_{0}+T_{1}}\varepsilon_{i,t}y_{j,t-k}\right|\geq a,\;\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T_{0}+T_{1}}y_{j,t-k}^{2}\leq b\right\}\leq 2\exp\left\{-\frac{a^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})b}\right\}.

Lemma S18

(Bounds for covariance matrices of stationary time series)
.

Suppose that Assumption
3
holds for
\{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\}
, and

\{\boldsymbol{y}_{t}\}
has the VMA(
\infty
) representation,
\boldsymbol{y}_{t}=\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*}(B)\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}
, where
\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*}(B)=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{j}^{*}B^{j}
,
B
is the backshift operator,
\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{0}^{*}=\boldsymbol{I}_{N}
, and
\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\|\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{j}^{*}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}<\infty
.
Let

\kappa_{1}=\lambda_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})\quad\text{and}\quad\kappa_{2}=\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*}),

where

\mu_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})=\min_{|z|=1}\lambda_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*}(z)\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*}^{{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{H}}}}(z))
,
\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})=\max_{|z|=1}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*}(z)\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*}^{{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{H}}}}(z))
, and
\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*}^{{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{H}}}}(z)
is the conjugate transpose of
\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*}(z)
.

(i)

It holds

\kappa_{1}\leq\lambda_{\min}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{y})\leq\lambda_{\max}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{y})\leq\kappa_{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\kappa_{1}\leq\lambda_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{y})\leq\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{y})\leq\kappa_{2}.

(ii)

Define the time series
\{\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\}
by

\boldsymbol{w}_{t}=\boldsymbol{U}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{U}_{i}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-i}
,
where
\boldsymbol{x}_{t}=(\boldsymbol{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\boldsymbol{y}_{t-2}^{\top},\dots)^{\top}
,
\boldsymbol{U}=(\boldsymbol{U}_{1},\boldsymbol{U}_{2},\dots)\in\mathbb{R}^{M\times\infty}
, and
\boldsymbol{U}_{i}
’s are
M\times N
blocks such that
\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\|\boldsymbol{U}_{i}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}<\infty
. Then,
\{\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\}
is a zero-mean stationary time series. Moreover,

\kappa_{1}\sigma_{\min}^{2}(\boldsymbol{U})\leq\lambda_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{w})\leq\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{w})\leq\kappa_{2}\sigma_{\max}^{2}(\boldsymbol{U})

(S62)

and

\lambda_{\max}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{w})\leq\kappa_{2}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\|\boldsymbol{U}_{i}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}\right)^{2}.

(S63)

Lemma S19
.

Suppose that the conditions in Lemma
S18
hold,
T_{0}
is a fixed integer, and
T_{1}
is a fixed positive integer.
For any
\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
and
\eta\geq 1
, if
\eta T_{1}\geq c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}^{-1}\log 2
, then

\mathbb{P}\left\{\forall j\geq 1:\frac{1}{T_{1}}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T_{0}+T_{1}}(\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-j})^{2}\leq\kappa_{2}(\eta j\sigma^{2}+1)\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}\geq 1-4e^{-c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}\eta T_{1}},

where
c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}>0
is the absolute constant in Lemma
S16
.

Lastly, the proof of Lemma
S5
also relies on Lemma
S20
below.
Let

\boldsymbol{\Xi}=\left\{\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N(r+2s)}\mid\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}\right\}\quad\text{and}\quad\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}=\{\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}\mid\|\boldsymbol{M}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}=1\},

where
\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi})
is defined as in Section
S5.1
. The following definition is used in Lemma
S20
.

Definition 1

(Generalized
\epsilon
-net of
\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
)
.

For any
\epsilon>0
, we say that

\boldsymbol{\bar{\Xi}}(\epsilon)
is a generalized
\epsilon
-net of
\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
if
\boldsymbol{\bar{\Xi}}(\epsilon)\subset\boldsymbol{\Xi}
, and for any
\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
, there exists
\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\bar{\phi}})\in\boldsymbol{\bar{\Xi}}(\epsilon)
such that
\|\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi})-\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\bar{\phi}})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\epsilon
. However,
\boldsymbol{\bar{\Xi}}(\epsilon)
is not required to be a subset of
\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
; that is,
\boldsymbol{\bar{\Xi}}(\epsilon)
may not be an
\epsilon
-net of
\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
.

Lemma S20

(Covering number and discretization for
\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
)
.

For any
0<\epsilon<1
, let
\boldsymbol{\bar{\Xi}}(\epsilon)
be a minimal generalized
\epsilon
-net of
\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
in the Frobenius norm.

(i)

The cardinality of
\boldsymbol{\bar{\Xi}}(\epsilon)
satisfies

\log|\boldsymbol{\bar{\Xi}}(\epsilon)|\leq(r+2s)\log\{3u_{\phi}/(l_{\phi}\epsilon)\},

where
l_{\phi}=(\sqrt{2}\overline{\alpha}_{\mathrm{MA}})^{-1}\min_{1\leq k\leq s}\gamma_{k}^{*}
and
u_{\phi}=\underline{\alpha}_{\mathrm{MA}}^{-1}
.

(ii)

For any
\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\bar{\Xi}}(\epsilon)
, it holds
l_{\phi}/u_{\phi}\leq\|\boldsymbol{M}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq u_{\phi}/l_{\phi}
.

(iii)

For any matrix
\boldsymbol{V}\in\mathbb{R}^{N(r+2s)\times T}
, it holds

\displaystyle\sup_{\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}}\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{V}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq(1-\epsilon)^{-1}\max_{\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\bar{\Xi}}(\epsilon)}\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{V}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}.

S8.9
Proofs of Lemmas
S16
–
S20

Proof of Lemma
S16
.

First it is obvious that
\{\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\}
is a zero-mean stationary time series. Without loss of generality, we let
T_{0}=0
and
T_{1}=T
in what follows.

Under Assumption
3
,
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{t}
, and all coordinates of the vector
\boldsymbol{\xi}=(\boldsymbol{\xi}_{T-1}^{\top},\boldsymbol{\xi}_{T-2}^{\top},\dots)^{\top}
are independent and
\sigma^{2}
-sub-Gaussian with mean zero and variance one.
In addition, by the vector MA(
\infty
) representation of
\boldsymbol{w}_{t}
, we have
\underline{\boldsymbol{w}}_{T}=\underline{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}^{w}\boldsymbol{\xi}
,
where

\displaystyle\underset{TM\times\infty}{\underline{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}^{w}}=\left(\begin{matrix}\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{1}^{w}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon}^{1/2}&\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{2}^{w}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon}^{1/2}&\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{3}^{w}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon}^{1/2}&\cdots&\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{T}^{w}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon}^{1/2}&\cdots\\
&\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{1}^{w}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon}^{1/2}&\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{2}^{w}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon}^{1/2}&\cdots&\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{T-1}^{w}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon}^{1/2}&\cdots\\
&&\ddots&&\vdots&&\\
&&&&\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{1}^{w}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon}^{1/2}&\cdots\end{matrix}\right).

Then, it holds

\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{w}=\mathbb{E}(\underline{\boldsymbol{w}}_{T}\underline{\boldsymbol{w}}_{T}^{\top})=\underline{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}^{w}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}^{w})^{\top}.

(S64)

Define the vector
\underline{\boldsymbol{m}}_{T}=((\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{w}_{T})^{\top},\dots,(\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{w}_{1})^{\top})^{\top}=(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{M})\underline{\boldsymbol{w}}_{T}
. Then
\underline{\boldsymbol{m}}_{T}=\boldsymbol{P}\boldsymbol{\xi}
, where
\boldsymbol{P}=(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{M})\underline{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}^{w}
. As a result,

\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}=\underline{\boldsymbol{m}}_{T}^{\top}\underline{\boldsymbol{m}}_{T}=\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}\boldsymbol{\xi}
. Similar to (
S64
), it follows from the Hanson-Wright inequality that
for any
\iota>0
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}-T\mathbb{E}\left(\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\right|\geq\iota\right)\leq 2\exp\left\{-c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}\min\left(\frac{\iota}{\sigma^{2}\|\boldsymbol{P}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}},\frac{\iota^{2}}{\sigma^{4}\|\boldsymbol{P}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}}\right)\right\}.

(S65)

By (
S64
), we have

\|\boldsymbol{P}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}=\|\boldsymbol{P}\boldsymbol{P}^{\top}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}\leq\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{M}^{\top}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}\|\underline{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}^{w}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}^{w})^{\top}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}\leq\lambda_{\max}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{w})\|\boldsymbol{M}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\trace(\boldsymbol{P}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P})=\trace(\boldsymbol{P}\boldsymbol{P}^{\top})

\displaystyle=\trace\{(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{M})\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{w}(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{M}^{\top})\}

\displaystyle=\textrm{vec}(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{M})^{\top}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{w}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{TQ})\textrm{vec}(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{M})\leq T\lambda_{\max}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{w})\|\boldsymbol{M}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2},

where the second equality follows from (
S64
). As a result,

\|\boldsymbol{P}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\sqrt{\|\boldsymbol{P}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}\trace(\boldsymbol{P}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P})}\leq\sqrt{\|\boldsymbol{P}\boldsymbol{P}^{\top}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}\trace(\boldsymbol{P}\boldsymbol{P}^{\top})}\leq\sqrt{T}\lambda_{\max}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{w})\|\boldsymbol{M}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}.

Taking
\iota=\eta\sigma^{2}T\lambda_{\max}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{w})\|\boldsymbol{M}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}^{2}
in (
S65
), the proof of this lemma is complete.
∎

Proof of Lemma
S17
.

By Assumption
3
,
\varepsilon_{i,t}
is
\sigma^{2}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})
-sub-Gaussian. Then, the result follows from Lemma 4.2 in
simchowitz2018learning
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
S18
.

Proof of (i):
Consider the spectral density of
\{\boldsymbol{y}_{t}\}
,

\boldsymbol{f}_{y}(\theta)=(2\pi)^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*}(e^{-i\theta})\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon}\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*}^{{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{H}}}}(e^{-i\theta}),\hskip 14.22636pt\theta\in[-\pi,\pi].

Let

\mathpzc{M}(\boldsymbol{f}_{y})=\max_{\theta\in[-\pi,\pi]}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{f}_{y}(\theta))\quad\text{and}\quad\mathpzc{m}(\boldsymbol{f}_{y})=\min_{\theta\in[-\pi,\pi]}\lambda_{\min}(\boldsymbol{f}_{y}(\theta))

Along the lines of
basu2015regularized
, it holds

2\pi\mathpzc{m}(\boldsymbol{f}_{y})\leq\lambda_{\min}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{y})\leq\lambda_{\max}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{y})\leq 2\pi\mathpzc{M}(\boldsymbol{f}_{y}),

2\pi\mathpzc{m}(\boldsymbol{f}_{y})\leq\lambda_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{y})\leq\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{y})\leq 2\pi\mathpzc{M}(\boldsymbol{f}_{y}),

and

\lambda_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*})\leq 2\pi\mathpzc{m}(\boldsymbol{f}_{y})\leq 2\pi\mathpzc{M}(\boldsymbol{f}_{y})\leq\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon})\mu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*});

(S66)

see Proposition 2.3 therein.
Thus, (i) is proved.

Proof of (ii):
Since
\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\|\boldsymbol{U}_{i}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}<\infty
and
\{\boldsymbol{y}_{t}\}
is stationary with mean zero, the time series
\boldsymbol{w}_{t}=\mathscr{W}(B)\boldsymbol{y}_{t}=\mathscr{W}(B)\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{*}(B)\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}
is also zero-mean and stationary, where
\mathscr{W}(B)=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{U}_{i}B^{i}
.

For any
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
, denote by
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{y}(\ell)=\mathbb{E}(\boldsymbol{y}_{t}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-\ell}^{\top})
the lag-
\ell
covariance matrix of
\boldsymbol{y}_{t}
, and then
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{y}(\ell)=\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\boldsymbol{f}_{y}(\theta)e^{i\ell\theta}d\theta
.
For any fixed
\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
with
\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}=1
,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{w}\boldsymbol{u}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{U}_{j}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-j}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}_{k}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-k}\right)\boldsymbol{u}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{U}_{j}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{y}(k-j)\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}_{k}\boldsymbol{u}

\displaystyle=\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\boldsymbol{U}_{j}\boldsymbol{f}_{y}(\theta)e^{-i(j-k)\theta}\boldsymbol{U}_{k}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u}\,d\theta

\displaystyle=\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\mathscr{W}(e^{-i\theta})\boldsymbol{f}_{y}(\theta)\mathscr{W}^{{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{H}}}}(e^{-i\theta})\boldsymbol{u}\,d\theta,

(S67)

where
\mathscr{W}(z)=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{U}_{j}z^{j}
for
z\in\mathbb{C}
, and
\mathscr{W}^{{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{H}}}}(e^{-i\theta})=\big\{\mathscr{W}(e^{i\theta})\big\}^{\top}
is the conjugate transpose of
\mathscr{W}(e^{-i\theta})
.
Since
\boldsymbol{f}_{y}(\theta)
is Hermitian,
\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\mathscr{W}(e^{-i\theta})\boldsymbol{f}_{y}(\theta)\mathscr{W}^{{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{H}}}}(e^{-i\theta})\boldsymbol{u}
is real for all
\theta\in[-\pi,\pi]
. Then it is easy to see that

\mathpzc{m}(\boldsymbol{f}_{y})\cdot\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\mathscr{W}(e^{-i\theta})\mathscr{W}^{{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{H}}}}(e^{-i\theta})\boldsymbol{u}\leq\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\mathscr{W}(e^{-i\theta})\boldsymbol{f}_{y}(\theta)\mathscr{W}^{{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{H}}}}(e^{-i\theta})\boldsymbol{u}\leq\mathpzc{M}(\boldsymbol{f}_{y})\cdot\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\mathscr{W}(e^{-i\theta})\mathscr{W}^{{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{H}}}}(e^{-i\theta})\boldsymbol{u}.

Moreover, since
\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}e^{i\ell\theta}d\theta=0
for any
\ell\neq 0
, we can show that

\displaystyle\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\mathscr{W}(e^{-i\theta})\mathscr{W}^{{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{H}}}}(e^{-i\theta})\boldsymbol{u}\,d\theta

\displaystyle=\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\boldsymbol{U}_{j}e^{-i(j-k)\theta}\boldsymbol{U}_{k}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u}\,d\theta

\displaystyle=2\pi\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\boldsymbol{U}\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u}.

which, together with the fact of
\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}=1
, implies that

\displaystyle 2\pi\sigma_{\min}^{2}(\boldsymbol{U})\leq\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\mathscr{W}(e^{-i\theta})\mathscr{W}^{{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{H}}}}(e^{-i\theta})\boldsymbol{u}\,d\theta\leq 2\pi\sigma_{\max}^{2}(\boldsymbol{U}).

(S68)

In view of (
S66
)–(
S68
), we accomplish the proof of (
S62
).

To verify (
S63
), note that the spectral density of
\{\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\}
is

\boldsymbol{f}_{w}(\theta)=\mathscr{W}(e^{-i\theta})\boldsymbol{f}_{y}(\theta)\mathscr{W}^{{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{H}}}}(e^{-i\theta}),\hskip 14.22636pt\theta\in[-\pi,\pi];

see Section 9.2 of
Priestley81
. Then

\displaystyle\mathpzc{M}(\boldsymbol{f}_{w})=\max_{\theta\in[-\pi,\pi]}\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{f}_{w}(\theta))

\displaystyle\leq\mathpzc{M}(\boldsymbol{f}_{y})\max_{\theta\in[-\pi,\pi]}\lambda_{\max}\{\mathscr{W}(e^{-i\theta})\mathscr{W}^{{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{H}}}}(e^{-i\theta})\}

\displaystyle=\mathpzc{M}(\boldsymbol{f}_{y})\max_{\theta\in[-\pi,\pi]}\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{U}_{j}e^{-ij\theta}\right\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathpzc{M}(\boldsymbol{f}_{y})\left(\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\|\boldsymbol{U}_{j}\|_{{\mathrm{op}}}\right)^{2}

In addition, by a method similar to the proof of Proposition 2.3 in
basu2015regularized
, we can show that

\lambda_{\max}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{w})\leq 2\pi\mathpzc{M}(\boldsymbol{f}_{w}).

Combining the above results with (
S66
), the proof of (
S63
) is complete.
∎

Proof of Lemma
S19
.

We first fix
j\geq 1
. Applying Lemma
S16
(ii) with
\boldsymbol{M}=\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}
and
\boldsymbol{w}_{t}=\boldsymbol{y}_{t-j}
, together with the result

\lambda_{\max}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{w})=\lambda_{\max}(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{y})\leq\kappa_{2}

as implied by Lemma
S18
(i), we can show that

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|\frac{1}{T_{1}}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T_{0}+T_{1}}(\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-j})^{2}-\mathbb{E}\{(\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-j})^{2}\}\right|\geq\eta j\sigma^{2}\kappa_{2}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}\leq 2e^{-c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}\min(\eta j,\eta^{2}j^{2})T_{1}}=2e^{-c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}j\eta T_{1}}.

holds for any
\eta>0
. In addition, by Lemma
S18
(i),

\mathbb{E}\{(\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-j})^{2}\}\leq\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{y})\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\kappa_{2}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}^{2}.

Thus, we further have

\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{T_{1}}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T_{0}+T_{1}}(\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-j})^{2}\geq\kappa_{2}(\eta j\sigma^{2}+1)\right\}\leq 2e^{-cj\eta T_{1}}.

By considering the union bound over all
j\geq 1
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left\{\exists j\geq 1:\frac{1}{T_{1}}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T_{0}+T_{1}}(\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\boldsymbol{y}_{t-j})^{2}\geq\kappa_{2}(\eta j\sigma^{2}+1)\right\}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}2e^{-cj\eta T_{1}}\leq 4e^{-c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}\eta T_{1}},

if
\eta T_{1}\geq c_{{\mathrm{HW}}}^{-1}\log 2
. The proof is complete.
∎

Proof of Lemma
S20
.

Proof of (i):
Note that if
\|\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}=1
, it follows from (
S14
) that

l_{\phi}\leq\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}\leq u_{\phi}
. This implies

\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}\subset\{\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\mid\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Pi}\}
,
where

\boldsymbol{\Pi}=\{\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\mathbb{R}^{r+2s}\mid l_{\phi}\leq\|\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{2}\leq u_{\phi}\}.

Hence, the problem of covering
\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
can be converted into that of covering
\boldsymbol{\Pi}
.

For any fixed
\epsilon>0
, let
\boldsymbol{\bar{\Pi}}(\epsilon)
be a minimal
(l_{\phi}\epsilon)
-net for
\boldsymbol{\Pi}
in the Euclidean norm. Denote

\boldsymbol{\bar{\Xi}}(\epsilon)=\left\{\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N(r+2s)}\mid\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\bar{\Pi}}(\epsilon)\right\}.

Thus, for every
\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
, there exists
\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\bar{\phi}})\in\boldsymbol{\bar{\Xi}}(\epsilon)
with
\boldsymbol{\bar{\phi}}\in\boldsymbol{\bar{\Pi}}(\epsilon)
such that
\|\boldsymbol{\phi}-\boldsymbol{\bar{\phi}}\|_{2}\leq l_{\phi}\epsilon
. By (
S14
), we further have

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi})-\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\bar{\phi}})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}=\|\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi}-\boldsymbol{\bar{\phi}})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\epsilon.

In addition, note that
\boldsymbol{\bar{\Xi}}(\epsilon)\subset\boldsymbol{\Xi}
. Therefore,
\boldsymbol{\bar{\Xi}}(\epsilon)
is a generalized
\epsilon
-net of
\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
.
Moreover, by a standard volumetric argument (see also Corollary 4.2.13 in
Vershynin2018
for details), the cardinality of
\boldsymbol{\bar{\Pi}}(\epsilon)
satisfy

\displaystyle\log|\boldsymbol{\bar{\Pi}}(\epsilon)|\leq(r+2s)\log\{3u_{\phi}/(l_{\phi}\epsilon)\}.

Noting that
|\boldsymbol{\bar{\Xi}}(\epsilon)|\leq|\boldsymbol{\bar{\Pi}}(\epsilon)|
, the proof of (i) is complete.

Proof of (ii):
Since

\boldsymbol{\bar{\Pi}}(\epsilon)\subset\boldsymbol{\Pi}
, we have

\boldsymbol{\bar{\Xi}}(\epsilon)\subset\left\{\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N(r+2s)}\mid\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\boldsymbol{\Pi}\right\}.

Then by (
S14
), for any
\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\bar{\Xi}}(\epsilon)
, it holds

l_{\phi}/u_{\phi}=\underline{\alpha}_{\mathrm{MA}}l_{\phi}\leq\|\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}\overline{\alpha}_{\mathrm{MA}}}{\min_{1\leq k\leq s}\gamma_{k}^{*}}u_{\phi}=u_{\phi}/l_{\phi}.

Thus, (ii) is proved.

Proof of (iii):
From the proof of (i), for every
\boldsymbol{M}:=\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi})\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
, there exists
\bar{\boldsymbol{M}}:=\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\bar{\phi}})\in\boldsymbol{\bar{\Xi}}(\epsilon)
with
\boldsymbol{\bar{\phi}}\in\boldsymbol{\bar{\Pi}}(\epsilon)
such that
\|\boldsymbol{M}-\bar{\boldsymbol{M}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}=\|\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi}-\boldsymbol{\bar{\phi}})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\epsilon
. In addition, since
\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi})
is linear in
\boldsymbol{\phi}
, we have
(\boldsymbol{M}-\bar{\boldsymbol{M}})/\|\boldsymbol{M}-\bar{\boldsymbol{M}}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}=\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi}-\boldsymbol{\bar{\phi}})/\|\boldsymbol{M}(\boldsymbol{\phi}-\boldsymbol{\bar{\phi}})\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
. Then for any
\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
, we can show that

\|\boldsymbol{M}_{(1)}\boldsymbol{V}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\|\bar{\boldsymbol{M}}\boldsymbol{V}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}+\|(\boldsymbol{M}-\bar{\boldsymbol{M}})\boldsymbol{V}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}\leq\max_{\bar{\boldsymbol{M}}\in\boldsymbol{\bar{\Xi}}(\epsilon)}\|\bar{\boldsymbol{M}}\boldsymbol{V}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}+\epsilon\sup_{\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}}\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{V}\|_{{\mathrm{F}}}.

Taking supremum over all
\boldsymbol{M}\in\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{1}
on both sides, we accomplish the proof of Lemma
S20
.
∎
